## [DART Enables Training-Free LoRA Reuse in Few-Step Video Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20051) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DART, a training-free method that combines low-rank coordinate transport with target-schedule response calibration to adapt LoRA adapters for few-step video diffusion models. On a four-step Wan2.2 target, DART-F improves the joint quality score from 0.9029 to 0.9227 and changes macro functional retention from -0.4644 to +0.1349. This addresses a practical bottleneck in efficient video generation: LoRA adapters trained for long denoising trajectories lose their intended effects when reused with distilled few-step models. The method could let creators reuse existing LoRA assets without retraining, lowering the cost of deploying fast video diffusion pipelines. Component analysis shows that response calibration accounts for most of the quality improvement, while coordinate transport provides complementary gains when combined with calibration. Adapter-level results reveal positive functional effects for some adapters and strong attenuation of negative effects for others, and evaluations on two additional targets show the same aggregate trend.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts large pretrained models by training small low-rank matrices instead of all weights. Step distillation compresses a multi-step diffusion model into a few-step generator to cut inference cost, but this changes the denoising schedule that a LoRA adapter was originally trained against. DART targets this mismatch without requiring source training videos or additional training.

Because anyone can edit it, the graph suffers from redundant classes, ambiguity between instances and classes, wrong taxonomic paths, and violations of type constraints, which are schema rules specifying which types of entities can participate in a given relation. Cleaning these issues manually is infeasible at Wikidata's scale, motivating automated approaches.

## [UnifiedPlayers: Cooperative Three-Player Framework for Tool-Integrated Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20089) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20089v1) introduces UnifiedPlayers, a cooperative framework with three specialized players — a Planning Player that generates tasks, an Execution Player that produces multi-turn trajectories with Python tool calls, and an Evaluation Player that builds executable verifiers — trained jointly under GRPO with role-specific rewards. Across two model backbones and twelve reasoning benchmarks, it outperforms the strongest prior baseline by at least 3.5% on mathematical reasoning and 3.9% on general reasoning, while its learned verifier reaches 84.2% adversarial detection accuracy. Existing self-evolving agent training typically separates trajectory generation from evaluation, relying on static verifiers that cannot adapt to new failure modes or self-consistency signals that may reinforce shared errors. By jointly adapting planning, execution, and evaluation, UnifiedPlayers points toward self-enhanced tool-integrated agents that generate and validate their own training data with less human annotation. The core technical challenge is coordination: each player continuously changes the data or feedback used to train the others, which the authors address with role-specific rewards under GRPO. The learned verifier's reward signal shows 2.03× higher per-question variance than a self-consistency baseline, yielding more discriminative verification, though the work remains a preprint without independent replication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning extends RL for large language models so that models act as autonomous agents making dynamic decisions rather than producing single static outputs. Tool-integrated reasoning lets reasoning models call external tools such as Python interpreters to go beyond text-only reasoning, and self-evolving agents aim to autonomously improve by generating their own training data. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm that estimates advantages from groups of sampled responses, commonly used to train reasoning models.

## [Decomposed Confidence Layer Enables Reliable Straight-Through Processing of Financial Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20110) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20110) introduces a decomposed confidence layer with three interpretable channels—perception, layout, and validation—combined with conformal risk control to enable reliable straight-through processing (STP) of financial documents using Vision Language Models (VLMs). Validated on three public datasets (real invoices, synthetic invoices, and ad-buy forms) with two VLM families (Qwen3.6-27B and Gemini-3.1-Flash-Lite), the method raises AUROC from 0.54–0.74 for native VLM verbalized confidence to 0.90–0.99, and auto-approves 49–72% of fields while holding empirical error at or below a 10% target, versus only 0.1–7.0% for native signals. Reliable STP is a long-standing goal in financial document automation, where even small error rates can cause costly downstream mistakes, and this work shows that VLM-based extraction can be made trustworthy enough for industrial deployment. By providing a bounded guarantee on residual error for the auto-approved tier, it could significantly reduce manual review workload in accounts payable, expense processing, and similar workflows. The decomposed score combines three interpretable channels—perception, layout, and validation—and applies conformal risk control to produce a calibrated probability with a bounded error guarantee on the auto-approved tier. The approach was tested across three datasets and two VLM families, consistently improving separation of correct from incorrect extractions, though the abstract does not detail computational overhead or performance on out-of-distribution document types.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Straight-through processing (STP) refers to handling financial documents such as invoices and expense reports end-to-end without human intervention, which requires high confidence in automated extraction. Vision Language Models (VLMs) can extract key-value fields from document images out of the box, but their self-reported confidence signals are poorly calibrated and only weakly correlate with actual correctness. Conformal risk control is a statistical framework that extends conformal prediction to bound the expected value of a loss function, providing distribution-free guarantees that are useful for setting safe auto-approval thresholds.

## [MTVA-Bench: A New Benchmark for Language Models in Cascaded Voice Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20152) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MTVA-Bench, a benchmark that evaluates the language model component of cascaded voice agents under realistic conditions such as transcription errors and multi-turn interactions. It contains 49 agents across 490 reviewed scenarios in 7 languages, using an LLM-simulated caller, a mock backend that responds to the model's actual tool-call arguments, and two LLM judges that must cite specific transcript messages. Existing evaluations either score the whole voice pipeline (mixing ASR and LLM errors into one number) or isolate the LLM without the difficulties of real phone calls, so MTVA-Bench fills a gap for developers who need to know whether failures come from the language model or the speech stack. Its seven-model study shows that tool selection accuracy is nearly identical across models while overall scores diverge by 24.4 points, suggesting that argument values, action ordering, and rule compliance are where real quality differences lie. Scoring combines deterministic checks on tool calls with two LLM judges—one grading scenario-specific rules and one grading conversation quality without access to the task—and task and conversation scores are weighted equally because a call can complete its task yet still go badly for the caller. The benchmark is limited to 490 reviewed scenarios and 7 languages, and the mock backend only responds to the arguments the model actually sent, so it tests decision-making rather than real API behavior.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Most production voice agents are cascaded systems: an ASR (automatic speech recognition) model transcribes the caller's audio, a language model reads the transcript and decides what to say and which backend tools to call, and a TTS (text-to-speech) model speaks the reply. Nearly all decision-making happens in the language model, but end-to-end voice benchmarks score the full pipeline while standard LLM benchmarks ignore phone-call realities like transcription errors, callers whose speech is split across multiple messages, and the requirement to reply in a specified language and script.

## [Sequential Contextual Fit Predicts Behavioural and Neural Dynamics Across Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20179) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20179) introduces sequential contextual fit (SCF), an embedding-based measure of how well a current information state matches its recent context, computed with a simple recency-weighted similarity kernel. Across language processing, music-evoked emotion, audiovisual emotion EEG, gambling decisions, human activity recognition, and decision-related EEG, lower SCF predicted longer processing times, larger affective or behavioural transitions, and stronger neural-state changes. The work offers a domain-general computational measurement layer that links contextual compatibility to behavioural processing and cognitive/neural state transitions, potentially unifying otherwise separate research programmes in psycholinguistics, affective science, decision-making, and neuroscience. Because the effects survive controls for established predictors such as surprisal and reinforcement-learning prediction error, SCF could become a standard tool for sequence-based cognitive modelling and for evaluating AI systems that process sequential information. SCF is computed with a recency-weighted similarity kernel and can be applied to words, sounds, visual scenes, affective states, choices, actions, and neural representations. Its predictive power held after controlling for surprisal, reinforcement-learning prediction error, acoustic change, visual change, and sensor change, though the study is a preprint and the analyses cover only a subset of the available audiovisual emotion EEG data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Embeddings are dense vector representations in which similar items (words, images, states) lie close together in a learned space, and they are widely used in natural language processing and cognitive modelling. Surprisal is an information-theoretic measure of how unexpected an event is given its context, and it is a dominant predictor of processing effort in language comprehension. Reinforcement-learning prediction error similarly measures the mismatch between expected and actual outcomes in decision-making. SCF generalizes this intuition by asking not how surprising an item is, but how well it fits the recent context as a whole.

## [JointMatch: Unified Graph Neural Solver for Ride-Sharing Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20200) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20200) introduces JointMatch, a learning-based framework that jointly optimizes request pairing and vehicle assignment on a single sparsified heterogeneous graph instead of solving them sequentially. On New York City Yellow Taxi data, it outperforms the classical Blossom heuristic and a two-stage GNN baseline, and at city scale (fleet of 10,000) runs over 20x faster per dispatch epoch. Ride-sharing platforms currently rely on a two-stage decomposition that commits to ride bundles before knowing which vehicles are available, losing revenue and scaling poorly. JointMatch shows that a single graph neural solver can capture both decisions at once, potentially improving both matching quality and dispatch throughput for large-scale mobility platforms. The graph is sparsified by spatial proximity so its size grows linearly rather than quadratically with the number of vehicles and requests, and a GNN scores all candidate decisions in one forward pass. A supervised training stage closes most of the remaining revenue gap, and a policy-gradient fine-tune aligns the model with realized revenue; the work is a preprint without peer review yet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Ride-sharing matching is typically split into request pairing (which open requests to bundle into shared trips) and vehicle assignment (which idle vehicle serves each bundle). Classical solvers like Edmonds' Blossom algorithm compute maximum matchings on general graphs but do not scale well to city-sized fleets, while graph neural networks (GNNs) learn to score decisions on graph-structured data and heterogeneous GNNs handle graphs with multiple node and edge types.

## [Cognitive Serializability: A Consistency Model for Committing AI Agent Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20261) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20261) proposes Cognitive Serializability, a formal consistency model that guarantees an autonomous agent's committed effects have a valid serial order and a logical point at which all derivation inputs—data, evidence, policy, and authority—remain unchanged. The authors also introduce a weaker Effect-Compatible Cognitive Admission model and a prototype system called TCT, which reportedly prevented all injected anomalies while adding only 3.22 ms mean commit overhead. As autonomous AI agents increasingly read databases, retrieve evidence, and act under delegated authority, there is no established guarantee that the inputs they reasoned over are still valid at commit time. This work bridges database isolation theory and agentic reasoning, offering a formal foundation that could influence how future agent runtimes, transaction systems, and policy engines ensure trustworthy autonomous actions. The framework uses typed dependency tokens to distinguish content integrity from applicability, and trusted mediation to capture the values exposed to reasoning. TCT combines immutable versioned executable definitions, registry-derived authority plans, sealed envelopes, guard-first commit transactions, co-committed receipts, and receipt-driven epistemic reconciliation, with results giving serializability conditions and an observational-equivalence boundary for zero-error soundness and positive progress.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: In database systems, isolation levels such as Serializable guarantee that concurrent transactions produce the same result as if they had run one after another, preventing anomalies like dirty reads or lost updates. AI agents complicate this picture because their actions are derived not only from database reads but also from retrieved evidence, policies, beliefs, and delegated authority, all of which can change mid-reasoning. Cognitive Serializability extends the classical serializability concept to cover this broader set of derivation inputs, ensuring a committed action has a coherent logical point of validity.

## [JEPA-WAM Adds Generated Visual Instructions to Robot World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20277) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20277) introduces JEPA-WAM, which augments each text instruction with a bank of stochastically generated task-completion images sampled from an off-the-shelf text-to-image generator, without training the generator. These images are encoded by a frozen V-JEPA 2.1 encoder into compact goal tokens that condition both the video and action experts via cross-attention, achieving 87.3%, 74.5%, and 80.9% success on a new real-robot benchmark and beating π0 and Fast-WAM by at least 10.0, 27.3, and 14.5 percentage points. The work targets a recognized structural imbalance in robot-learning data, where rich visual-action trajectories are paired with sparse, repetitive language annotations, causing policies to infer tasks from visual context rather than truly grounding instructions. If validated, this approach could meaningfully improve instruction-following in embodied AI and offer a lightweight way to reuse existing text-to-image models for robot control. The generated images may differ from the current scene in appearance and layout but remain semantically aligned with the instruction, serving as visual goal references; the frozen V-JEPA 2.1 encoder focuses on task-level semantics rather than appearance. The benchmark covers in-distribution, out-of-distribution scene, and out-of-distribution instruction settings, though the results come from a preprint without peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: World Action Models (WAMs) are a class of robot foundation models that augment pretrained video generative models with action experts, learning both how the world changes and what actions a robot can take. JEPA (Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture) is a self-supervised framework that learns by predicting abstract representations of inputs rather than reconstructing raw pixels. JEPA-WAM combines these ideas by using JEPA latent representations to connect generated visual instructions to WAM control.

## [AgentPProf: Semantic Profiler for Long-Horizon AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20301) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AgentPProf proposes a semantic operation stack model that replaces the runtime call stack with uniform operations and operation stacks, enabling hierarchical attribution of agent activity by task intent rather than code paths. It aggregates agent trajectories into pprof-compatible profiles for flame graph visualization, achieving 0.764 B³ F1 against human annotations on CodeTraceBench and raising MAP by up to 56% on three problem-localization benchmarks. Existing agent observability tools focus on per-execution debugging and tracing, leaving developers unable to answer cross-run questions about where failures happen, what triggers unsafe effects, and which tasks consume the most budget. By bringing classic systems profiling concepts to agent trajectories, AgentPProf could help teams improve agent quality, safety, and token cost efficiency at scale. The core technical idea is recursive operation segmentation, which exploits the observation that an agent's task occupies a contiguous span and decomposes into subtasks, recursively splitting trajectories at task boundaries. The profiler outputs pprof-compatible profiles for flame graph analysis, and the code is available at https://github.com/eunomia-bpf/agentsight.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: In systems software, profiling aggregates resource consumption and attributes it to responsible code paths to identify hotspots, complementing per-execution debugging. AI agents increasingly orchestrate long-running activities with users, tools, and system resources over days or weeks, but their responsible entities are task intents like "diagnose authentication" or "compare branches" rather than stable code paths, making traditional profiling hard to apply. pprof is a widely used profiling format and toolchain that supports flame graph visualization of hierarchical resource usage.

## [New Bayesian Framework Diagnoses Hidden Dynamics Changes Before Task Reveal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20304) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20304) introduces the problem of 'task readiness under dormant dynamics drift,' where an agent must detect hidden component-level changes, recover control policies, and certify readiness before future task identity is known. The authors propose Evidence-Gated Matched-Pulse Transport (EG-MPT), a two-stage Bayesian intervention-based method that couples fault localization with actuator effectiveness estimation through a shared matched-response representation. This work addresses a critical gap in safety-critical control systems, where a deployed policy can mask actuator degradation that is irrelevant to the current task but essential for future tasks. The framework enables deployment decisions that explicitly trade off expected performance, confidence, and fallback use, with potential impact on robotics, autonomous systems, and safe AI. EG-MPT operates under a limited, task-agnostic interaction budget, using a small number of informative probes to characterize changes before downstream task identity is revealed. It provides each candidate task with either a recovered policy and a calibrated lower bound on achievable return or an abstention decision to a safe fallback, and is evaluated on dormant-actuator benchmarks across multiple simulators using readiness coverage, selective risk, and interaction cost metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: In control theory and robotics, a deployed control policy may not excite certain actuators, so a loss of actuator effectiveness can remain dormant and undetected until a future task requires that actuator. Traditional fault diagnosis assumes the task is known or that faults manifest immediately, while this paper formalizes a new decision problem that unifies active fault diagnosis with post-change control recovery under a limited interaction budget. Bayesian methods are commonly used for fault diagnosis because they provide principled uncertainty quantification, which is essential for certification and safe deployment decisions.

## [NeuSOGA3D: Neuro-Symbolic Framework for Explainable 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20323) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced NeuSOGA3D, a hybrid neuro-symbolic framework that reconstructs 3D geometry from unorganized point clouds by projecting them onto principal orthographic planes and building symbolic implicit spline representations. It fuses these via shape-preserving constructive solid geometry (CSG) operations to form a coarse visual hull, then recovers finer detail through cross-sectional decomposition and volumetric reconstruction using Partial Shape-Preserving Splines, achieving CAD-compatible outputs across all 40 ModelNet40 categories. Unlike conventional neural implicit methods that encode geometry in opaque latent representations, NeuSOGA3D produces explicit symbolic entities such as control polygons and implicit spline fields, making reconstructions interpretable and reusable in engineering and CAD workflows. This could advance explainable geometric intelligence and bridge the gap between deep learning-based perception and symbolic reasoning in design and manufacturing. The framework progressively transforms observations into explicit symbolic entities, including control polygons, implicit spline fields, cross-sections, and volumetric lofts, rather than relying solely on latent codes. Experiments cover all forty categories of the ModelNet40 benchmark, though the paper is a preprint without peer review, so its immediate impact and generalization beyond the benchmark remain to be validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Neuro-symbolic AI combines neural networks with symbolic reasoning to create more robust and trustworthy systems, addressing deep learning's limitations in reliability and interpretability. Neural implicit methods for 3D reconstruction encode geometry in latent representations that achieve high accuracy but are hard to interpret or reuse. Constructive solid geometry (CSG) is a solid modeling technique that builds complex shapes by combining simpler primitives with Boolean operators, commonly used in CAD and procedural modeling.

## [S4L Framework Translates Traffic Rules into Prolog with 75% Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20334) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Structured Four-Stage Legal Translation (S4L→Prolog), a reasoning-guided framework that converts raw natural-language traffic rules into executable Prolog code without human intervention. Evaluated on a benchmark of twenty real-world traffic rules, S4L correctly formalized 75% of them, outperforming the NL→Prolog baseline (60%) and the LE→Prolog baseline (55%). This work addresses a core challenge in legal AI: the gap between ambiguous human language and the precision required by computational reasoning engines. If structured reasoning prompts can reliably improve natural-language-to-logic translation, it could enable automated compliance checking and safety-critical legal reasoning systems. The S4L framework performs semantic role extraction, scene completion, logical mapping, and Prolog rule generation within a single guided prompt. Qualitative analysis shows it captures implicit causal relations, deontic modality, and exception structure more reliably than the baselines, though the benchmark is limited to twenty traffic rules.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Prolog is a declarative logic programming language rooted in first-order logic, widely used in AI and automated theorem proving, where knowledge is expressed as facts and rules. Traffic regulations are written for human interpretation and rely on shared background knowledge and flexible phrasing, which introduces ambiguity and semantic underspecification that conflict with Prolog's demand for explicit logical structure. Semantic role labeling is an NLP task that assigns labels such as agent, goal, or result to words or phrases, helping identify who did what to whom. Logical English is a controlled natural language used to express rules in a form closer to formal logic.

A new arXiv paper (2609.20358) introduces a hybrid generative model that combines denoising diffusion with adversarial training to reconstruct heterogeneous 3D geological microstructures from 2D images. By overcoming the difficulty that GAN-based methods like SliceGAN have with complex heterogeneous microstructures, the approach could improve property simulation in materials science and geological waste disposal. It generates microstructures of varying complexity with minimal slice artefacts and close agreement with ground-truth phase fractions and structural descriptors.

## [SkillAA: Attribution-Guided Skill-Graph Updating for Frozen LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20455) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SkillAA (Skill Abductive Attribution) is a new structured skill-optimization framework for frozen language models that represents skill applicability, execution, and composition in a unified graph, enabling skill selection, attribution-guided repair, and update validation within the same structure. Using gpt-5.6-sol, it achieves 81.5%, 66.7%, and 91.2% on SearchQA, LiveMath, and DocVQA respectively, attaining the highest observed mean in every main setting. This work addresses a key limitation of existing external-skill methods, which edit skills directly from failed rollouts without structured routing from an observed failure to an editable location. By enabling targeted, validated, and reversible skill updates without parameter updates, SkillAA could improve how frozen language models acquire and refine domain procedures, benefiting AI/ML researchers working on model editing and skill optimization. SkillAA contrasts successful and failed executions to route candidate repairs to specific graph objects, updates only the selected local structure, and uses Local and Big Gates to screen candidate changes before commitment. The framework also leverages semantic boundaries, object addresses, and topological dependencies for skill retrieval, targeted updating, and scoped validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Frozen language models are models whose parameters are not updated during downstream use, so they must rely on external skills or tools to handle domain-specific procedures. Skill graphs are structured representations that capture skills and their interrelations, and abductive attribution is a reasoning approach that infers the most likely explanation for an observed outcome. SkillAA combines these ideas to diagnose failures and repair skills in a structured, scoped manner.

## [Planning Guidance and Terminal Verification in Stateful LLM Agent Harnesses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20474) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study in τ²-bench shows that prewritten task-specific plans (Fixed) improve oracle-verified success by 7.17 percentage points over word-count-matched shuffled policy text (Sham) across 265 matched cells, with gains concentrated in higher-complexity tasks. A read-only terminal verifier rejects 61% of Retail oracle-invalid episodes while withholding 17% of correct ones, at less than one cent of additional cost per episode. The results give agent builders actionable guidance: which harness component matters more depends on the loss assigned to erroneous acceptance, and a standalone verifier captures nearly all the false-pass benefit of the full planning-plus-verification stack at a fraction of its cost. This helps practitioners decide where to invest when building stateful LLM agents for customer-service-style tasks. The primary comparison isolates guidance content by matching Fixed plans against shuffled policy text of equal word count, and the 7.17-point gain carries a 90% task-clustered bootstrap interval of 1.15–13.36 points. At low liability for erroneous acceptance the planning gain dominates, while at high liability the verifier's avoided false passes dominate.

Oracle-verified success means task completion judged against a ground-truth reference rather than a cheaper LLM-as-a-judge proxy.

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20519) introduces SoL-Pi, an RSI-inspired method that recursively scales auto-research loops across many diverse environments to discover reusable agent harness mechanisms. On the 51-task EdgeBench evaluation, SoL-Pi matches Pi's performance across GPT-5.6 Sol and Opus 5 while cutting recorded token traffic by 44.7-49.0% and API cost by roughly one third. Token efficiency is becoming a bottleneck for scaling unattended, around-the-clock coding agents, so a harness-layer method that preserves performance while roughly halving token traffic could directly lower the cost of running long agent trajectories. The estimated hourly savings of $8.75-$13.50 versus native Codex and Claude Code harnesses, and $4.36-$5.71 versus Pi, suggest practical value for teams deploying agents at scale. Four mechanisms survive the selection process and form SoL-Pi, covering action execution, context compaction, observation handling, and delegated reading. The work is an arXiv preprint with no peer review yet, and the reported savings are estimates based on recorded token traffic on EdgeBench rather than production deployments.

Recursive self-improvement (RSI) refers to systems that use experience and feedback to persistently improve their own capabilities and their future improvement process. EdgeBench is a benchmark designed to measure how autonomous agents learn from real-world environments over long horizons, making it a fitting testbed for harness-level efficiency research.

## [FreqCondNorm: Frequency-Conditioned Transformer for Cross-Domain Predictive Maintenance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20535) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FreqCondNorm introduces a FiLM-style frequency-conditioned normalization layer within a Transformer architecture, pretrained on five public predictive maintenance datasets (CWRU, MFPT, UOC18, PRONOSTIA, CMAPSS) using masked auto-encoding and contrastive learning with balanced domain sampling. It achieves 99.2% fault diagnosis accuracy on CWRU (+6.4 pp over CNN) and 82.1% zero-shot accuracy on MFPT, but does not improve remaining useful life (RUL) prediction. Predictive maintenance models often fail to transfer across machines and operating conditions, especially when signals span five orders of magnitude in sampling frequency (1 Hz to ~100 kHz). FreqCondNorm's frequency-conditioned normalization offers a way to unify heterogeneous time-series in a single foundation model, potentially reducing the need for labeled data in industrial fault diagnosis. The model uses a FiLM-style conditioning mechanism where frequency information modulates normalization layers, enabling a single model to handle signals at vastly different sampling rates. While fault diagnosis improves significantly, the paper honestly reports that RUL prediction does not benefit, suggesting a mismatch between pretraining objectives and RUL tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Predictive maintenance uses sensor data to detect faults and predict equipment failure. Deep learning models struggle to generalize across different machines because vibration signals vary widely in sampling frequency and operating conditions. FiLM (Feature-wise Linear Modulation) is a general conditioning method that modulates neural network activations based on external context, and masked auto-encoding is a self-supervised pretraining technique that reconstructs masked portions of input data.

## [LLM agent groups overstate consensus versus human deliberation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20543) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20543) replays 100 held-out human Wason deliberation groups with matched LLM agent groups, seeding one belief-anchored agent per participant's pre-discussion answer and scoring both with identical code. Across two sensitivity analyses, LLM groups showed full-consensus rates 34.0–44.4 percentage points higher than humans, with the gap persisting under early-stopping removal and answer-memorization reparameterization. This is a rigorous methodological critique of using LLM agent groups as proxies for human collective cognition, showing that simulated consensus is a biased estimator of human deliberative outcomes. It matters for AI evaluation and multi-agent systems research, warning that high agreement among LLM agents should not be read as evidence of genuine collective reasoning or accuracy. Human full-consensus estimates ranged from 24.0% to 57.0% depending on scoring definition, and about one fifth of human participants never posted while agents almost always did. The submit-based comparison (n=98) gave gaps of 34.0 and 43.9 points for chat and reasoning modes, the participation-matched comparison (n=45) gave 34.1 and 44.4 points, and the two routes converged within 0.5 points; reasoning-mode groups agreed nearly unanimously, mostly on incorrect answers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: The Wason selection task is a classic logic puzzle devised by Peter Cathcart Wason in 1966 to test deductive reasoning, in which people must decide which cards to turn over to test a conditional rule. Researchers increasingly use groups of LLM agents to simulate human group deliberation, treating full consensus as a marker of collective cognition. This paper instead replays real human deliberation data with matched agent groups to test whether that simulation is faithful.

## [Paper Proves Confidence-Based Discrete Diffusion Sampling Fails on Dependent Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20581) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20581) proves that discrete diffusion samplers—including remasking and uniform-state samplers—can only match the training distribution when the positions written in a step are conditionally independent given already-fixed tokens. The authors show that no product of per-position distributions can match a dependent group, and they validate this on ScanAndAdd, a synthetic task with a closed-form joint distribution, measuring a total variation of 29× the sampling-noise floor while per-sample metrics remain at 1.0. This is a fundamental theoretical limitation for a widely used generation paradigm: per-position confidence ranking, which is standard in masked discrete diffusion language models, cannot in principle reproduce distributions with token dependencies. It suggests that current confidence-based samplers are structurally flawed for real domains like text, pixels, and phonemes, and points toward the need for joint samplers or trainable correction layers. The proof shows that per-position distributions do not determine whether a group is dependent—two joint distributions can share identical per-position marginals yet differ in which value combinations occur—so confidence scores carry no information about dependence. On ScanAndAdd, every group of two or more undetermined positions selected by a confidence ranking is dependent, and the resulting distribution deviates far from the target even though per-sample metrics look perfect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Discrete diffusion models are a class of generative models that generate sequences by iteratively denoising a discrete state space, and they are commonly used for language modeling. In masked or remasking variants, the model writes several token positions per step, drawing each token from a per-position distribution and using those same distributions to decide which positions to fill. Confidence-based sampling, which fills the positions the model is most confident about, is a standard and efficient strategy, but it assumes that positions can be treated independently.

## [PAA: A Probabilistic Extension of Allen's Interval Algebra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces the Probabilistic Allen Algebra (PAA), a generative and complete probabilistic extension of Allen's interval algebra in which relation probabilities are derived from distributions over interval boundaries rather than assigned as scores. Time points are modeled as Gaussian, intervals have Gaussian midpoints and truncated-Gaussian durations, and all thirteen relations form a true partition under a single tolerance band, recovering crisp Allen as the tolerance vanishes. This addresses a significant gap in temporal reasoning under uncertainty, where natural-language expressions like "just before" or "roughly during" have graded meaning that crisp predicates cannot capture. It could benefit researchers in AI, NLP, knowledge representation, and databases who need principled probabilistic temporal inference. Point-point relations reduce to error functions, while point-interval and interval-interval relations become multivariate Gaussian orthant probabilities induced by linear inequalities; contact relations (meets, starts, finishes, equals) receive positive measure through a tolerance band. The algebra is scale-invariant, decomposes each relation into correlation-aware temporal primitives in the spirit of CIDOC CRM, and is shipped as an open, MIT-licensed, tested Python package (import name paa).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Allen's interval algebra, introduced by James F. Allen in 1983, is a qualitative calculus that defines thirteen mutually exclusive and jointly exhaustive binary relations between time intervals, along with a composition table for reasoning about temporal descriptions of events. Its relations are crisp predicates over exact interval boundaries, which is inadequate when times, durations, and boundaries are uncertain. PAA instead derives Allen's taxonomy from probability distributions over boundaries, using concepts such as multivariate Gaussian orthant probabilities—the probability that all components of a multivariate Gaussian are nonnegative.

## [Study: Ownership in AI-Assisted Tasks Depends on How You Collaborate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20658) by Megan Wei, Melanie Subbiah, Audrey Lee, Annya Dahmani, Dave Edwards, Helen Edwards, and Ellie Pavlick reports an exploratory qualitative survey in which participants described two recent AI-assisted tasks — one that felt like their own and one that did not. The study finds that people disown work when they merely approve AI suggestions, but retain ownership when they lead, iterate, or rewrite, and that loss of personal voice and lack of comprehension both erode ownership. As AI becomes woven into everyday tasks, this work offers empirical grounding for HCI and AI ethics discussions about authorship, credit, and disclosure, and could influence how AI tools are designed to preserve users' sense of contribution. It also highlights that willingness to disclose AI use is often decoupled from actual pride or ownership and is instead shaped by community norms and fear of credit erasure. The study is an exploratory qualitative survey based on self-selected tasks, so its findings are not statistically generalizable; ownership can also extend to settings where people own the vision but not the execution, and respondents reported high ownership on tasks they could not have completed without AI. The authors propose several research directions to promote AI development that supports people's sense of authorship over their own lives.

**Background**: Qualitative surveys are a common HCI method for studying complex, socially situated phenomena that resist quantitative measurement, using techniques such as identifying critical incidents and categorizing themes and patterns. This paper applies that approach to human-AI collaboration, asking when work done with AI still feels like one's own, a question relevant to debates about AI credit, disclosure norms, and tool design.

## [Deep Noir autonomously discovers activation steering parameters in transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20722) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Deep Noir, a new framework, autonomously discovers optimal activation steering parameters using Logit Lens convergence and causal head-level attribution, achieving a 16.7 percentage-point improvement on spam detection at 1B scale and 21-42 points at 7-9B across four architectures, plus a 13.1-point gain on SST-2 sentiment with zero code changes. It also reveals that steering creates a predictable prompt-injection attack surface that grows monotonically with steering magnitude. This work automates a previously manual, trial-and-error process of controlling LLM behavior at inference time, making activation steering more practical and generalizable across tasks and architectures. The discovery of a predictable prompt-injection attack surface has direct security implications for agent systems that deploy steered classifiers. The framework was evaluated across three scales (1B x 3, 2-3B x 2, and 7-9B x 4) with 39 runs on the spam task, and on sentiment it outperformed RepE without head masking, which failed to improve over baseline, with all Deep Noir improvements significant at p < 0.01. The prompt-injection vulnerability increases monotonically with steering magnitude, a caveat relevant to safety-critical deployments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Activation steering is a technique that modifies a language model's internal activations at inference time to change its behavior without retraining, but choosing where and how strongly to steer has traditionally been a manual process. Logit Lens is an interpretability method that projects intermediate hidden states into vocabulary space to show how a model's predictions evolve layer by layer, while causal head-level attribution assigns responsibility to specific attention heads by measuring their causal effect on outputs. Deep Noir combines these two techniques to automatically locate effective intervention points, addressing a key bottleneck in mechanistic interpretability and LLM control.

Researchers posted arXiv:2609.19199v1, introducing Code-as-Auditor, an LLM-based framework that converts regulatory text into formalized checklists and executable decision trees. During inference, each checklist item is expanded into factual and counterfactual questions, and a self-verification loop checks the logical consistency of the generated code and the traceability of outcomes. LLM-based legal and compliance reasoning often lacks explicit grounding in legal logic and evidence, which limits trust and auditability. By encoding regulations as interpretable code structures, this approach could make automated compliance checking more accurate, evidence-backed, and traceable for privacy and data protection scenarios. The framework operates in two stages: regulation-to-code translation into checklists and decision trees, then inference-time expansion into factual and counterfactual questions that guide evidence identification, rule application, and final decision-making. Experiments on privacy and data protection scenarios show more accurate and evidence-backed evaluations, though the paper is an arXiv preprint without peer review.

## [REACT: Fully Spiking State-Space Model for Real-Time Event-Driven Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19204) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose REACT, a fully spiking state-space model that processes raw event camera data one event at a time without temporal accumulation, using a complex-valued spiking neuron called C-SiLIF whose continuous-time dynamics are driven by the physical inter-event interval. On the EvTTC benchmark, REACT achieves 9.59% relative time-to-collision error with 4.6 ms end-to-end latency, within 0.15 percentage points of the best learned method while requiring no target bounding box or localization prior. This work shows that event-driven spiking state-space dynamics can deliver low-latency, continuously updated temporal perception, which is critical for reactive robotic systems operating in dynamic environments. The 4.6 ms latency corresponds to only 4 cm of vehicle motion at the dataset's mean approach speed, compared with 1 m for the fastest competing learned method, potentially enabling safer and faster collision avoidance. REACT supports anytime time-to-collision prediction, zero-shot transfer to a different driving sequence, and INT8 quantization, which reduces estimated energy consumption from 18.5 to 2.8 mJ per 32,768 events. It is evaluated on gesture recognition and TTC estimation from full-field event streams without a target bounding box or localization input.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Event cameras are neuromorphic sensors that asynchronously report per-pixel brightness changes with microsecond resolution, rather than capturing frames at a fixed rate. Spiking neural networks mimic biological neurons by transmitting information as discrete spikes only when a membrane potential crosses a threshold, incorporating time into their operation. State-space models represent a system's dynamics through latent state variables that evolve over time, and have been adapted in AI for sequential data modeling. REACT combines these three ideas to process raw events one by one without the integration delay introduced by accumulating events into frames or temporal bins.

## [MeshKV: A NoC-Based KV Cache Fabric for Transformer Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19207) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present MeshKV, a KV cache fabric that moves cache blocks as packetized flows over a lightweight network-on-chip (NoC) for tiled transformer decoding accelerators. It combines TaKV affine striping, Mare multicast with verified duplicate suppression, and Pad prefetching, achieving up to 58% less interconnect traffic, 2.1x better KV bandwidth utilization, and up to 1.9x multi-stream throughput on an 8x8 FPGA implementation with LLaMA-2-7B and Mistral-7B at 8K-32K context lengths. Long-context LLM serving is increasingly bottlenecked by irregular KV cache movement on centralized memory paths, so rethinking the cache as a distributed NoC fabric could unlock higher throughput for tiled accelerators. This hardware-software co-design offers a concrete path to scale autoregressive decoding without simply adding DRAM bandwidth. The design converts bisection back-pressure into useful KV transfer through credit-aligned FIFOs that overlap prefetch, tile multiply, and streaming softmax. Results are from an 8x8 FPGA prototype rather than silicon, and the evaluation covers only two 7B-class models at 8K-32K context, so scaling to larger models and longer contexts remains to be shown.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive transformer decoding caches previously computed keys and values (the KV cache) so each new token avoids recomputing attention over the whole sequence, but this cache grows with context length and must be shuttled between memory and compute tiles. Tiled accelerators split work across many small compute units, and a network-on-chip (NoC) is a router-based packet-switched interconnect that replaces shared buses to move data between those units. MeshKV applies NoC principles to KV cache traffic, using affine striping to spread cache homes and multicast to share blocks across tiles.

## [Homophily-Aware Stratification Proposed for Stable GNN Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19210) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19210) argues that class stratification alone is insufficient for graph neural network evaluation and proposes Homophily-Aware Stratification (HAS), a topology-aware procedure that treats node homophily as the primary stratification axis with class label as a secondary axis. Evaluated on 15 node-classification datasets and 7 GNN architectures, HAS achieves a mean stability rank of 1.49 versus 2.31 for random k-fold, with the lowest mean stability rank on 13 of 15 datasets. This work addresses a core methodological flaw in GNN evaluation: random splits vary in homophily distribution, inflating reported variance and making published architecture comparisons unreliable. Broader adoption of homophily-aware split construction could improve reproducibility and fairness in graph learning research, affecting how benchmarks are designed and how results are compared across the field. HAS aligns folds with respect to local relational consistency alongside the class marginal that standard stratification already controls, and because stratifying on homophily alone does not guarantee class balance, it incorporates class label as a secondary axis. The method preserves class balance close to class-stratified splits and substantially better than random splits, though the abstract does not report per-dataset accuracy numbers or computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Graph neural networks (GNNs) are widely used for transductive node classification, where the full graph including unlabeled test nodes is available during training and accuracy is measured on randomly drawn train/validation/test splits. Homophily measures the tendency of connected nodes to share the same label; high-homophily graphs like citation networks have neighbors likely sharing labels, while low-homophily graphs like Amazon co-purchase graphs may not. In non-graph settings, stratified k-fold cross-validation is the classical remedy that ensures each fold reflects the full class distribution, but this paper argues that class stratification alone ignores the relational conditions that drive message-passing behavior in GNNs.

## [PRESLEY Adds Generative AI Layers to Video Streaming for 56.4% Bitrate Cut](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19215) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PRESLEY, a new arXiv preprint, extends the earlier ELVIS pipeline by replacing destructive block removal with adaptive in-place degradation under a removability mask, signaling per-block strength in a bit-packed side channel and restoring content via generative backbones conditioned on transmitted visual priors. It achieves a mean -56.4% BD-rate reduction over its predecessor across 13 rate ladders spanning multiple codecs and dataset families, and up to -29.4% BD-rate savings versus pristine baselines in bit-starved regimes. The work shows that generative AI layers can meaningfully reduce bandwidth while preserving perceived quality, potentially reshaping how streaming services balance bitrate, quality, and compute. It also maps the theoretical headroom of this architecture class, giving researchers a roadmap for joint rate-distortion-restoration selection rules. PRESLEY keeps foreground fidelity bit-exact while improving background quality in 17 of 23 sequences, and an exact leave-one-superblock-out combinatorial oracle shows existing complexity heuristics already capture 83.3% of bit-cost savings, bounding remaining cost-axis headroom at about 5% of total bitrate. The primary unaddressed axis, post-restoration damage, disperses widely (4.9-8.4 dB) but is predictable before transmission with a held-out rho of +0.400.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional video codecs treat every region of a frame alike, spending bits uniformly regardless of where viewers actually look. ELVIS, the predecessor conference work, introduced an end-to-end pipeline that removes redundant blocks server-side and reconstructs them client-side using generative in-painting. PRESLEY builds on this by degrading blocks in place rather than deleting them, and by conditioning restoration on transmitted visual priors instead of unconditioned in-painting. BD-rate is a standard metric measuring bitrate savings at equal quality, where negative values indicate improvement.

## [PAPC: Platform Mediation for Privacy-Propagation Externalities in AI Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19226) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19226v1) introduces PAPC, a platform-mediated mechanism that intercepts information-moving events—such as memory writes, shared-workspace updates, inter-agent messages, and tool events—before they update shared state or external channels in AI-mediated workflows. Across retrieval-memory and multi-agent workflow benchmarks, PAPC preserves deterministic task completion while eliminating measured exact raw-value and external raw-value exposure. Current privacy controls focus on final outputs, missing intermediate disclosures that impose downstream exposure costs on other principals; PAPC reframes event-level mediation as a platform-governance primitive for agent-mediated online work. This matters for researchers and practitioners building multi-agent systems and privacy-preserving AI, where high-fanout objects can amplify propagation. PAPC combines policy, provenance, topology/fanout, privilege, and content signals to allow an event, release a policy-safe abstraction, quarantine raw content, block a transition, or narrow onward rights. The model explains why final-output control misses intermediate exposure costs and why high-fanout objects amplify propagation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: AI-mediated platforms coordinate work through LLM agents acting for different principals, and privacy loss can occur before a final answer appears. A privacy-propagation externality is a failure mode where the cost of a raw disclosure depends on topology and fanout as well as content. PAPC is presented as a platform-mediation mechanism for multi-principal AI-mediated workflows that intercepts information-moving events before they update shared state or reach external channels.

## [Traffic-Aware Conformal Prediction Boosts Intrusion Detection Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19241) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.19241) proposes traffic-aware conformal prediction, which calibrates thresholds on traffic drawn from the attacker's expected perturbation mechanism, and attack-orbit invariance, which excludes attacker-controllable features and their deterministic descendants from the scored representation. The authors demonstrate that standard conformal prediction fails under adversarial perturbation across three intrusion detection benchmarks, and that their orbit-invariance approach yields an exact, pathwise coverage guarantee while remaining unchanged under every evaluated attack attempt. Intrusion detection systems increasingly rely on machine learning, but their predictions lack statistical validity guarantees and are vulnerable to adversarial manipulation of network features. This work bridges conformal prediction and adversarial robustness, offering provable coverage guarantees that could make ML-based network security defenses more trustworthy in adversarial settings. The matched-calibration guarantee provably restores coverage when the attacker's perturbation mechanism is known and samplable, but a stronger adaptive attacker that queries the target model's own score can still degrade it. The orbit-invariance representation remains completely unchanged under every evaluated attack across three independently fine-tuned language model architectures, at a quantified cost of seven to fourteen points in clean accuracy relative to the unrestricted feature set.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a distribution-free uncertainty quantification technique that produces prediction sets with finite-sample coverage guarantees, assuming exchangeability of data. Intrusion detection systems monitor network traffic to identify malicious activity, and machine learning models used for this task are known to be vulnerable to adversarial examples that perturb input features. Orbit invariance is a property where a function remains constant on group orbits, often used in provably invariant neural network architectures.

## [Physics-Informed Deep Learning Reconstructs 3D Coronary Hemodynamics from Angiography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19290) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19290) proposes a physics-informed hemodynamic modeling framework that reconstructs 3D coronary geometry from dual-view angiography using an attention-enhanced CNN, then predicts velocity and pressure fields with a decoupled network embedding physical priors. Across 32 clinical patients under four flow conditions, it achieved a trans-stenotic pressure-drop MAPE of 2.02%, velocity and pressure relative-L2 errors of 0.054 and 0.023, and 93.8% diagnostic accuracy (30/32) against hospital-measured FFR, with the full pipeline running in under 20 minutes per patient. This work addresses two long-standing bottlenecks in coronary intervention planning: angiography's lack of depth information and FFR's single functional index, while avoiding the computational cost of numerical CFD and the heavy supervision and physical inconsistency of purely learning-based methods. If validated more broadly, such a data-free prediction and sparse-data assimilation pipeline could bring detailed hemodynamic insight into routine clinical decision-making for stenosis assessment and revascularization planning. The framework maps reconstructed point clouds to a reference domain and Fourier-encodes them for joint representation, with decoupled velocity and pressure prediction enabling efficient transfer across physiological conditions. The reported diagnostic accuracy of 93.8% comes from a small cohort of 32 patients (exact 95% CI 79.2%–99.2%), so the confidence interval is wide and larger multi-center validation is still needed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Fractional flow reserve (FFR) is a diagnostic technique used during coronary catheterization that measures the pressure ratio across a stenosis to assess whether a narrowing limits oxygen delivery to the heart muscle; an FFR of 0.80 means a 20% pressure drop. Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) are a class of deep networks that embed governing physical laws, typically partial differential equations, into training as a regularization mechanism, improving generalization when data are scarce. This paper combines both ideas, using PINN-style physical constraints to predict coronary blood flow fields from angiography instead of relying on expensive computational fluid dynamics simulations or large labeled datasets.

## [GAVEL uses graph world models to verify and repair LLM robot plans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GAVEL, a new framework built around an explicit graph world model, verifies and repairs long-horizon LLM-generated robot plans before execution. On BEHAVIOR-1K, it raises single-task success with Qwen3-8B from 41.2% to 91.8% and multi-task success from 19.9% to 92.6%, while distributional belief reasoning cuts travel distance by about 5.4%. LLM planners often produce plans that violate robot embodiment constraints or fail under partial observability, which limits their deployment in real embodied systems. GAVEL shows that pairing LLMs with an explicit graph world model can substantially improve reliability and efficiency, potentially making LLM-driven robot planning practical for long-horizon household and multi-task settings. The graph encodes object relations, action preconditions and effects, and probabilistic beliefs over unobserved object locations, letting GAVEL predict action consequences, detect violations, and repair errors that follow directly from the model while reserving LLM replanning for semantic errors. For multi-task instructions, it reorders subtasks over distributions of possible object locations to minimize expected search cost.

Partial observability means the robot cannot see the full state, such as where objects are, so plans must reason under uncertainty. A world model is an internal representation that predicts how actions change the environment, and graph-based world models represent objects and their relations as nodes and edges.

## [AUDITPLAN: Plan-Then-Answer Safety Alignment with FAITHGATE Reward Gating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19325) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AUDITPLAN proposes a single-model plan-then-answer safety alignment method in which the model first emits a compact structured safety plan recording a threat label, intended action, and explicit constraints, then answers conditioned on that plan. It is trained with supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning using FAITHGATE, a reward-gating objective that grants answer reward only when the safety plan is correct, and on Qwen2.5-3B-Instruct it reduces ASR from 24.0% to 11.6%, LSR from 1.0% to 0.36%, and over-refusal from 11.0% to 2.0%. Current safety tuning pipelines judge only the final answer, making it hard to distinguish robust refusal from blanket refusal on benign requests or polished but unfaithful safety rationales that do not actually constrain the answer. By making the internal safety commitment explicit and machine-checkable, AUDITPLAN points toward safety alignment that is simultaneously more faithful, robust, and auditable, which matters for anyone deploying or regulating language models. The safety plan remains hidden from users at deployment, so the approach adds no visible output overhead while enabling auditing, and FAITHGATE outperforms answer-only RL, free-form explanation, and weighted-sum structured rewards. Similar trends hold for Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct, and confirmation runs on Qwen-3-4B-Instruct and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct preserve the same trend, though the evaluation is limited to Qwen backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Safety alignment for large language models typically means fine-tuning or reinforcement learning so the model refuses harmful requests while still answering benign ones, and success is usually measured by attack success rate (ASR) and over-refusal rate. A persistent problem is that a model can produce a safe-looking answer or a plausible safety rationale without that reasoning actually causing the safe behavior, a gap sometimes called unfaithful safety. AUDITPLAN addresses this by having the model commit to a structured plan before answering, so the plan can be checked independently of the final response.

## [A-RAM: LLM Agent Framework Plans Kinematically Feasible Robotic 3D Printing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19347) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.19347) introduces A-RAM, an agent-specialist-tool framework in which an LLM interprets manufacturing objectives and constraints, encodes them into a schema-constrained request, and a deterministic Planning Agent then runs a search workflow with domain tools that compute slicing, placement, inverse kinematics, trajectory timing, Joint-6 jerk, and extrusion evidence. Evaluated on a six-axis robotic-arm AM cell across three case studies, selected plans achieved up to 53.5% lower maximum Joint-6 jerk and 48.3% lower mean absolute Joint-6 jerk than the least favorable valid candidates, while objective-specific infill screening cut motion-plan completion time by up to 40.1% and extrusion path length by up to 12.7%. Robotic additive manufacturing makes process planning robot-dependent, so a slicer plan that looks good in part coordinates can be infeasible or mechanically unfavorable on a manipulator; A-RAM addresses this gap by coupling LLM-based reasoning with pre-execution kinematic evaluation. If validated beyond the preprint stage, this kind of integrated planning could reduce trial-and-error, manual tuning, and failed prints for researchers and engineers using robotic-arm AM systems. The framework separates an LLM that identifies prescribed and searchable planning variables from a deterministic Planning Agent that instantiates the search workflow, keeping the generated plans traceable and execution-ready. Reported gains are relative to the least favorable valid candidates within the evaluated candidate sets, and the results come from three case studies on a single six-axis robotic-arm AM cell, so broader generalization and physical validation remain open questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Robotic additive manufacturing uses a multi-axis robotic arm instead of a conventional gantry-style 3D printer, which allows printing beyond flat, planar layers but makes the process plan depend on the robot's kinematics. Slicing software decides layer paths and part orientation, and those decisions interact with where the part is placed in the workspace and whether the arm can actually reach and move along the path without excessive joint jerk. Existing AM tools, LLM-based decision-support methods, and digital-shadow systems do not provide integrated pre-execution evaluation of these coupled decisions, which is the gap A-RAM targets.

## [Probe Guidance Boosts Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19356) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces 'probe guidance,' a method that uses the frozen internal states of an existing diffusion model to construct a guidance signal for flow matching models. Applied to a 1.7B diffusion language model, it sets a new state-of-the-art on unconditional generation and consistently improves multiple-choice QA benchmarks. This offers a practical, efficient alternative to autoguidance by eliminating an extra forward pass at inference time, potentially reducing compute costs for guided generation. It also provides new insight into why autoguidance works, which could influence future guidance design in diffusion and flow matching models. Probe guidance works on a principle similar to autoguidance but ensures the weak and strong models share similar dynamics without an additional forward pass. The paper also finds that in traditional autoguidance, the weak model must come from a low-entropy region of training to be effective.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a simulation-free, ODE-based generative modeling framework that learns time-dependent velocity fields to transform noise into data. Diffusion language models apply iterative denoising to text, generating tokens in parallel rather than sequentially like autoregressive models. Autoguidance improves sample quality by using a weaker version of the model itself as a guidance signal, but it typically requires an extra forward pass at inference.

## [Riemannian-Lorentz Fusion Merges ViTs and State-Space Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19384) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.19384) introduces Riemannian-Lorentz Parameter Fusion (RLPF), a method that merges independently trained Vision Transformers (ViTs) and State-Space Models (SSMs) by aligning parameter groups by semantic role and computing a regularized geodesic barycenter on the Lorentz hyperboloid. The fine-tuned hybrid system achieves 82.37% on CIFAR-10, 75.04% on Oxford-IIIT Pet, and 78.58% top-1 on ImageNet-1K, surpassing the best individual parent models (76.54%, 71.42%, and 76.42% respectively). Model merging offers orders-of-magnitude savings versus retraining, but existing weight-space methods assume shape-compatible checkpoints; RLPF tackles the harder case of heterogeneous architectures, potentially enabling reuse of diverse pre-trained models without gradient descent. If validated, this geometry-aware approach could reduce training costs and resource concentration in deep learning. RLPF projects aligned parameter groups to common coordinates, lifts selected coordinates to the Lorentz hyperboloid model of hyperbolic space, computes a regularized geodesic barycenter, and decodes the result into two branches; a learned gate then combines branch logits per input. Component groups use fixed curvature values with normalization parameters treated as Euclidean, and the authors note that RLPF is a two-branch hybrid whose gate and final models are trained, not a training-free single-checkpoint merge.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) process images by splitting them into patches and applying self-attention, while State-Space Models (SSMs) are sequence models that have recently gained traction in deep learning for efficient long-range modeling. Model merging aims to combine pre-trained checkpoints without gradient descent, but merging different architectures is challenging because their token-mixing operators and parameter shapes differ. Hyperbolic geometry, particularly the Lorentz model, provides a mathematically efficient way to represent hierarchical relationships and compute geodesics, which RLPF leverages to align and fuse parameters.

## [RWPS Cuts Nash Equilibrium Simulation Cost in Cybersecurity Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19399) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Regret-Weighted Payoff Sampling (RWPS), a budgeted estimator that simulates only the payoff-matrix cells an equilibrium is sensitive to and fills the rest with a surrogate model trained on previously simulated entries. The authors prove an instance-dependent error bound weighted by the opponent's equilibrium mixture, plus a coverage result showing surrogate error cannot affect either player's regret once the deviation-relevant set is simulated. Payoff estimation via Monte-Carlo rollouts of slow simulators is the main bottleneck in PSRO-based equilibrium computation, so a method that provably focuses simulation budget where it matters could make large cybersecurity games tractable. On three 21x21 general-sum games the refined bounds are four to six times tighter, and RWPS achieves lower exploitability than minimum-regret-first search, information-gain search, and progressive sampling at matched budgets. The standard sup-norm error bound cannot evaluate RWPS because it is dominated by cells deliberately left inaccurate, so the authors instead prove an instance-dependent bound and a certificate computable from simulation data alone. The coverage result predicts in advance which games are cheap: only 18% of the matrix for small-support games versus 82% for the asymmetric Colonel Blotto game, and RWPS is lowest on the CyGym and ANSG cyber simulators at the smallest budgets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Policy-Space Response Oracles (PSRO) is a game-theoretic framework that computes approximate Nash equilibria in large games by iteratively adding best responses to a restricted empirical game, but each payoff-matrix entry requires expensive Monte-Carlo rollouts of a slow simulator. Nash equilibrium is the standard solution concept in which no player can improve by unilaterally changing strategy, and in cybersecurity games it is used to model attacker-defender interactions where players randomize to avoid being outguessed. RWPS addresses the payoff-estimation bottleneck by combining targeted simulation with surrogate modeling.

## [HALTER: Graph-Based Harness Automates Long-Horizon Robot Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19413) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present HALTER, a Harness for Autonomous Long-horizon Task Evaluation and Reset that restores robot scenes by planning over a library of learned atomic reset skills instead of relying on a single reset policy. On four long-horizon Franka arm tasks, HALTER restores the scene in 76% of episodes versus 52% for AutoEval and 65% for motion-planning reset, estimates completed-skill fraction correctly in 90% of episodes versus 76%, and cuts operator time by 72% relative to manual reset. Real-robot evaluation of manipulation policies is bottlenecked by human resets and unspecified initial states, which makes results hard to reproduce. By automating reset, scoring, and verification for long-horizon tasks, HALTER could make robot learning evaluation faster, more reproducible, and scalable, directly benefiting robotics and embodied AI researchers. HALTER builds a spatial scene graph online from point clouds and vision foundation models, and an LLM reasons over this graph to score rollouts, plan resets, and verify reset success without collecting labeled success images. It also demonstrates compositional generalization on three held-out tasks, resetting 74.7% of episodes versus 1.3% for a per-task reset policy, and includes ablations of the scene representation and graph update rate.

A new arXiv survey (2609.19445) introduces the first structured model-to-system taxonomy for Efficient Multimodal Learning (EML), distilling insights from over 300 works into three hierarchical levels: model, algorithm, and system. It also provides an integrative case study of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) and posits a paradigm shift toward self-regulating intelligence where efficiency is an intrinsic design property. It can help researchers and practitioners navigate the fragmented EML landscape and design natively efficient, deployable multimodal systems. The taxonomy covers architectural parsimony at the model level, execution refinement at the algorithm level, and hardware-aware orchestration at the system level, while explicitly analyzing the 'Efficiency-Utility-Privacy' trade-off. ## [DASE Query Engine Links Unstructured Data for Multi-step LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19491) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.19491) introduces DASE, a query engine that jointly executes multi-attribute filtering, multi-vector search, exact relational joins, and thresholded embedding-similarity joins for LLM and AI agent workflows. DASE combines a multi-step reasoning query model, SemJI (a sparse materialized embedding-similarity join index for rare near-neighbor pairs), and a co-designed execution layer using predicate-aware ANN traversal, batched access, and threshold-based score aggregation. Retrieval pipelines for LLM agents increasingly need to combine structured predicates, vector similarity, and relational joins in one query, which today's RDBMS, rerank, and vector-database stacks handle poorly. DASE reports 6x to 46x faster candidate retrieval at comparable recall, and as a high-recall prefilter it improves BigQuery quality on SemBench E-Commerce from 0.67 to 0.80 while cutting cost from $2.42 to $0.54, suggesting cheaper and more accurate downstream LLM evaluation. The system targets scientific-discovery workloads and assumes a planned query with a monotone scoring function, so it is not a general-purpose database replacement. SemJI is specifically designed for rare near-neighbor pairs, and the execution layer relies on predicate-aware approximate nearest neighbor traversal plus threshold-based score aggregation rather than exhaustive exact search.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Embedding-similarity joins match records whose vector representations are close, and they are a core operation when LLMs must gather evidence from unstructured text. Approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) search trades a small amount of accuracy for large speed gains in high-dimensional vector spaces, while sparse materialized indexes store only a small subset of entries to keep index size manageable, as seen in systems like ClickHouse. DASE's contribution is co-designing these techniques into a single engine that also respects relational joins and structured filters.

## [Isolated LLMs Struggle to Coordinate Covertly, One Frontier Model Excels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19504) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces 'For Your Eyes Only,' a cooperative signalling game in which a Sender model produces free-form descriptions for two words (one a hidden target) and an isolated Receiver instance of the same model must identify the target using only shared pre-training and task instructions. Evaluating seven contemporary models from four architectural families on 300 word pairs from psycholinguistic corpora with a Double-Pass Success Rate metric, the authors find most models fail to maintain coordination once detectable signals are filtered out, while one frontier model retains near-perfect performance. As automated workflows increasingly route model-generated content between independent model instances, the ability to embed and detect covert signals without shared memory or coordination-specific training becomes a practically important capability. The finding that coordination is consistently weaker across architectures than within them suggests current multi-agent deployments may face reliability gaps when mixing different models. The study uses 300 word pairs drawn from established psycholinguistic corpora and applies the Double-Pass Success Rate to control for output biases, ensuring results are not artifacts of response tendencies. The authors also demonstrate that models can direct this covert coordination capability toward deliberate misdirection, raising safety considerations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Emergent communication research in multi-agent systems studies how autonomous agents develop shared protocols without human intervention, often using cooperative signalling games where a sender and receiver must agree on a referent. Large language models are typically trained independently, so any coordination between separate instances must arise from shared pre-training rather than explicit joint training or shared memory. This paper tests whether such zero-shot covert coordination is possible in natural language.
