Researchers introduced Atria Dawn Preview, a foundation agentic language model for scientific research and engineering workflows, trained via a Verifiable Experience Pipeline that links tool-mediated interactions to executable environments and externally verified outcomes. Across 16 benchmarks spanning real-world research, engineering, and digital work, it is competitive with frontier agents and achieves the highest reported score on five of them. The human-AI collaboration study analyzed 769 task records from 56 participants alongside agent logs; when evaluating completed tasks under comparable conditions, participants rated about one-third of completed AI-assisted tasks as infeasible without AI. Benchmarks are standardized test suites used to compare model performance across tasks.

## [Graph-Based NLI Achieves 89.7% Accuracy with Interpretable Pipeline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.16814v3) introduces a fully graph-based natural language inference pipeline in which the classifier never directly processes input text; instead, sentences are decomposed into atomic propositions, converted into ConceptNet triples via constrained decoding, and represented as three graphs (premise, hypothesis, and a retrieved ConceptNet subgraph) that are fed into a fine-tuned 0.8-billion-parameter language model. The pipeline achieves 89.7% accuracy on SNLI, just 1.9 points below an identically trained text-based model, and matches RoBERTa-large on ANLI rounds R2 and R3, though it trails by 16 points on R1. This work addresses the lack of auditable decision structures in LLM-based NLI by making the reasoning process fully interpretable through graph representations, potentially enabling more trustworthy and debuggable NLP systems. The finding that graphs and text are complementary—combining both modalities achieves 92.1% accuracy on SNLI—suggests a promising hybrid direction for future interpretable AI. The authors term the performance gap on ANLI (9 to 14 points overall compared to the text counterpart) the 'price of interpretability' and demonstrate through ablations that it stems from representational limitations rather than data constraints. The model uses constrained decoding to convert atomic propositions into ConceptNet triples, and the 0.8B parameter size is notably smaller than typical LLM-based NLI systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Natural Language Inference (NLI), also known as Recognizing Textual Entailment (RTE), is the task of determining whether a hypothesis is entailed by, contradicts, or is neutral with respect to a premise. The Stanford Natural Language Inference (SNLI) corpus is a widely used benchmark of 570k human-written English sentence pairs labeled for this three-way classification. ConceptNet is a knowledge graph that connects words and phrases with labeled, weighted edges representing common-sense assertions. Atomic propositions are minimal, self-contained statements that capture a single fact or claim from a sentence.

<a id="item-211"></a>
## [Inverse Knowledge Distillation Boosts Adversarial Transferability via Information Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17003) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes Inverse Knowledge Distillation (IKD), an attack-agnostic mechanism that maximizes the prediction-distribution discrepancy between benign and adversarial samples on a surrogate model to improve adversarial transferability. It proves that soft-label cross-entropy and KL divergence induce identical gradients and Hessians under a fixed-anchor implementation, and derives a quantitative lower bound on dominant Fisher-subspace overlap between surrogate and target models. Transfer-based black-box attacks are a major practical threat to deployed deep learning systems, and IKD offers a lightweight, plug-in component that consistently improves black-box success rates across CNN, ViT, and defended models on ImageNet. Its theoretical analysis connects surrogate Fisher sensitivity to cross-model transfer, providing a principled foundation for future transferability research. IKD integrates seamlessly with standard gradient-based attacks without modifying their optimization pipelines, and ablations confirm the CE/KL equivalence and the pronounced disadvantage of mean squared error, which uses a different Euclidean pullback in output probability space. The analysis establishes a sufficient target-margin crossing condition under oriented gradient coherence and target smoothness, and code is available at https://github.com/ImmortalTing/IKD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial examples are inputs perturbed to fool neural networks, and transferability refers to the ability of adversarial examples crafted on a surrogate model to also deceive a different, unseen target model. Knowledge distillation typically trains a student network using soft labels from a teacher, but IKD inverts this idea by pushing adversarial predictions away from a fixed benign anchor. The Fisher information matrix measures the sensitivity of a model's output distribution to parameter changes, and its dominant subspace indicates directions that strongly influence predictions.

## [FORGE: Forensic Reasoning with Grounded Evidence for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15867) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FORGE introduces a novel architecture that routes a second visual stream from a Vision-Only Model (VOM) trained on dense patch prediction into a multimodal large language model (MLLM), enabling region-grounded natural language explanations for deepfake forensics. The method uses a two-stage adapter training protocol and a shared patch grid to interleave tokens, outperforming in-domain baselines on cross-domain evaluations for both face-manipulated and fully synthetic content. This work addresses a fundamental inductive bias in MLLMs that causes them to miss localized forensic cues, potentially improving the reliability and verifiability of deepfake detection in security and legal contexts. By enabling fine-grained, region-referential explanations, FORGE could help investigators and the public better understand and trust AI-generated content analysis. The VOM operates on a shared patch grid with the MLLM's native encoder, and interleaving their tokens with preserved spatial correspondence outperforms naive concatenation. The two-stage adapter training (generic image-caption alignment followed by joint task-specific optimization) prevents overfitting to training-domain manipulations, and region-specific evaluation and human studies confirm explanation faithfulness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) integrate text, images, and other data types to perform reasoning tasks, but their visual encoders are typically trained with image-text contrastive learning that optimizes for whole-image semantic summaries rather than patch-level details. Deepfake forensics requires detecting subtle local manipulations, such as altered facial features or synthetic artifacts, which are often missed by global representations. FORGE addresses this by adding a vision-only model trained on dense patch prediction, a technique common in dense prediction tasks like segmentation, to provide localized forensic evidence.

## [FOCAL uses optimal transport for fine-grained ECG-report alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11939) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FOCAL introduces an optimal-transport-based contrastive learning framework that aligns localized ECG waveform segments with individual report tags, rather than relying only on global representations. It also adds a semantic similarity matrix to reduce false negatives and an LLM-driven coarse-to-fine pipeline to recover missing waveform descriptions, achieving state-of-the-art zero-shot prediction and linear probing across six datasets. Most existing ECG-report contrastive models operate at the global level, which blurs the link between specific waveform regions and pathologies and limits interpretability. By enabling tag-level alignment, FOCAL could improve zero-shot ECG interpretation and make automated diagnosis more precise and clinically useful. The method addresses the fact that nearly 55% of standard clinical reports, such as those in MIMIC-ECG, lack explicit waveform descriptions, and it uses a coarse model verification step to filter out LLM hallucinations. Fine-grained tag-level alignment can worsen the false negative problem among reports sharing common diagnoses, which the semantic similarity matrix is designed to correct.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Electrocardiograms (ECGs) are non-invasive tools for diagnosing cardiovascular diseases, and multimodal contrastive learning has recently been used to align ECG signals with clinical reports for zero-shot interpretation. Optimal transport is a mathematical framework, related to the Wasserstein distance, that finds the most efficient way to match two probability distributions, making it well suited to pairing waveform segments with report tags. Contrastive learning trains models to pull matching pairs together and push mismatched pairs apart in a shared representation space.

## [AntiGrounding Uses Rendered Robot Trajectories as Visual Prompts for VLM-Guided Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AntiGrounding is a visual action-selection framework that renders candidate robot trajectories as images and feeds them to a vision-language model (VLM) for instruction-conditioned evaluation. Across eight real-world manipulation tasks, it achieves 71.25% overall success with a single GPT-6 Astra evaluator, versus 50.00% for pi0.5 and 47.50% for a PIVOT-style baseline. The work addresses a core challenge in embodied AI: translating natural-language instructions into executable robot trajectories. By connecting general-purpose multimodal reasoning to motion planning, it suggests a practical path for leveraging large VLMs in real-world manipulation without end-to-end fine-tuning. After feasibility filtering, each retained short trajectory serves both as an explicit motion plan and as a rendered prompt; structured multi-view visual question answering (VQA) scores safety, task alignment, efficiency, and physical plausibility, and weighted view fusion aggregates the scores. An initialized digital twin provides the planning state and validates selected segments before the real robot executes the same waypoint sequences, with performance bounded by digital-twin fidelity and physical interaction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal systems that can interpret images together with natural-language instructions, and visual question answering (VQA) is the task of answering open-ended questions about an image. In robotics, a digital twin is a real-time virtual copy of a physical robot that mirrors its state and environment, allowing plans to be tested before hardware execution. AntiGrounding combines these ideas by using rendered trajectory images as VQA prompts to select actions for manipulation tasks.

## [Spherical Cauchy VAE Enables Exact KL and Fast Sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21278) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the spherical Cauchy distribution as a heavy-tailed posterior for spherical variational autoencoders, using stereographic projection to map it to a multivariate Student law and a Möbius transformation to generate exact posterior samples from uniform spherical draws. It achieves exact KL divergence evaluation against the uniform prior, with a closed-form expansion that terminates in even ambient dimensions and certified truncation in odd dimensions, and reports 1.5x faster per-step evaluation than Power Spherical and 4.2x faster than robust von Mises-Fisher on CPU at dimension 128. This provides the sphere with a heavy-tailed posterior option comparable to the Student-t in Euclidean VAEs, removing the need for modified Bessel functions or rejection sampling that plague von Mises-Fisher and the antipodal density vanishing of Power Spherical. It could improve generative modeling for directional data such as 3D rotations, molecular conformations, and viewpoint-aware vision tasks, while lowering regularization cost at equal local precision. The KL divergence to the uniform prior reduces to a scalar expectation with one logarithm and a polynomial with finitely many correction terms; odd dimensions admit certified truncation of value and gradient, and the KL is increasing and convex in concentration. At matched modal curvature, the spherical Cauchy has broader angular tails and a smaller KL penalty than both alternatives, and across five paired seeds it attains the lowest MNIST reconstruction loss at every tested dimension and lowers held-out viewpoint-gap negative log-likelihood on smallNORB by 3.6%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Variational autoencoders (VAEs) learn latent representations by optimizing a reconstruction loss plus a KL divergence regularizer, and in Euclidean space heavy-tailed posteriors like the Student-t are common. On the sphere, the von Mises-Fisher distribution requires modified Bessel functions and rejection sampling, while the Power Spherical distribution achieves closed forms only by forcing density to vanish at the antipode. Stereographic projection maps the sphere onto a plane, and Möbius transformations are angle-preserving rational maps that can rotate the sphere, enabling exact sampling and tractable density evaluation.

## [Gaze Signals Reveal Role Switching in Human-AI Chess Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20666) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2509.20666v2) reports a study with 21 chess players completing over 1,100 decisions in a hand-and-brain chess task, showing that when participants switched roles with an AI partner they exhibited more exploratory gaze patterns and lower subsequent move quality. A classifier trained on gaze and task-specific features distinguished switch from stay decisions with a PR-AUC of 0.56, versus a random baseline of 0.39. This work suggests that intelligent systems could use passive behavioral cues, such as gaze, to detect when users want to reallocate responsibilities during collaboration, enabling more adaptive and responsive AI partners. It contributes empirical evidence to the growing field of human-AI teaming and could inform the design of future collaborative interfaces in HCI. The study used hand-and-brain chess, where on each turn a participant either selected the piece type (brain) while the AI chose the move (hand), or chose the move after the AI selected the piece type; players generally retained their roles across turns. Feature-set ablations showed that gaze and task-specific features contributed most to model performance, and interviews revealed that many participants perceived selecting the piece type as giving them greater control, with perceived AI ability, relative subtask difficulty, and desired influence shaping role preferences.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Hand-and-brain chess is a team variant where one player, the "brain," announces a piece type and the other, the "hand," moves any legal piece of that type, requiring close coordination with limited communication. In human-AI collaboration, tasks are often divided into complementary subtasks, and users may want to shift which parts they perform as task demands and their perception of the AI's capabilities evolve. This paper investigates whether multimodal behavioral signals, such as gaze, can reveal such role-switching decisions during sequential decision-making.

The paper introduces BUILD-BENCH, a challenging benchmark for evaluating LLM agents on compiling diverse real-world open-source software, and proposes OSS-BUILD-AGENT, a strong baseline agent with an enhanced build instruction retrieval module that achieves state-of-the-art performance on the benchmark. This benchmark addresses the limitations of prior selective evaluations by including OSS with more diverse quality, scale, and characteristics, and is expected to drive further research in automated software engineering and software security. BUILD-BENCH includes OSS projects where compilation instructions are often absent, dependencies are undocumented, and successful builds may require patching source files or modifying build scripts; OSS-BUILD-AGENT uses a two-stage, multi-agent system with LLM-assisted build instruction retrieval and iterative error repair.

BUILD-BENCH aims to provide a more realistic evaluation by including diverse OSS projects.

## [LLM Agents Collaborate Under Information Asymmetry via Communication and Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.25595) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2510.25595v2) extends the classic Einstein Puzzles into a table-top game where two LLM agents must reason, communicate, and act to satisfy spatial and relational constraints under information asymmetry. The authors apply a fine-tuning-plus-verifier framework, equipping agents with different communication strategies (asking, sharing, or both) and environment-based verification signals, with code released on GitHub. This work addresses a relatively underexplored angle of LLM agents—collaboration under information asymmetry—rather than the usual single-agent action planning, and shows that aligned communication and environment-based verification improve rule comprehension, safety, and interpretability. These findings are relevant to anyone building multi-agent LLM systems for tasks where agents hold private knowledge and must coordinate. Interestingly, agents without any communication can still achieve high task performance, but further analysis reveals they lack true rule understanding and receive lower trust from human evaluators; adding an environment-based verifier improves rule comprehension and task completion. The framework specifically studies configurations where agents possess both information-seeking and information-providing capabilities, highlighting the importance of aligned communication.

Information asymmetry refers to situations in multi-agent systems where different agents hold different private information, so they must communicate to reach a shared solution. Fine-tuning adapts a pretrained LLM to a specific task, while a verifier checks outputs against environment signals to catch errors.

## [In-Domain Self-Supervised Pre-Training Boosts Robustness to Noisy Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.20844) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2511.20844v2) shows that pre-training a feature extractor on the target dataset with in-domain self-supervised learning (SSL) — without any labels — followed by standard supervised training on the same noisy dataset yields a more noise-robust model. The approach works with both contrastive and non-contrastive SSL methods and eliminates the need for a clean-label subset, consistently improving classification accuracy and downstream label-error detection (F1 and Balanced Accuracy) across all noise rates. Most existing methods for learning with noisy labels depend on having a clean subset of data, which is often unavailable or expensive to obtain in real-world settings. By removing this requirement and showing broad applicability across large-scale datasets, diverse downstream tasks, and model architectures, this approach could make robust training practical for domains like medical imaging or web-scraped data where label noise is pervasive. The performance gap between in-domain SSL pre-training and supervised training from scratch widens as the noise rate increases, and the method achieves results comparable to ImageNet and DinoV2 pre-trained models at low noise levels while substantially outperforming them under high noise conditions. Evaluations cover both synthetic and real-world label noise.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Self-supervised learning (SSL) is a paradigm where a model generates supervisory signals from the data itself — for example, by creating augmented views of the same sample — rather than relying on human-provided labels. Contrastive learning, one popular SSL family, trains representations by pulling similar (positive) pairs together and pushing dissimilar (negative) pairs apart. Label noise, where training labels are incorrect, causes deep networks to overfit and generalize poorly, and prior remedies typically assume access to a clean subset for validation or sample selection.

## [MAS-Shield: A Three-Stage Defense Framework for Secure LLM Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22924) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers have proposed MAS-Shield, a defense framework for LLM-based multi-agent systems (MAS) that uses a coarse-to-fine, three-stage filtering pipeline: Critical Agent Selection, Light Auditing, and Global Consensus Auditing. Experiments show it achieves a 92.5% recovery rate against diverse adversarial scenarios while reducing defense latency by over 70% compared to existing methods. LLM-based multi-agent systems are increasingly deployed for complex collaborative tasks, but linguistic attacks can trigger cascading failures across the entire network. MAS-Shield addresses the fundamental security-efficiency dilemma by dynamically allocating defense resources, potentially enabling more practical and secure deployment of multi-agent AI systems. The framework strategically targets high-influence nodes to narrow the defense surface, uses lightweight sentry models to rapidly filter benign cases, and escalates only suspicious signals to a heavyweight committee for definitive arbitration. This hierarchical design optimizes the security-efficiency trade-off, though the work is currently an arXiv preprint without peer review.

Existing defenses face a dilemma: lightweight single-auditor methods risk single points of failure, while robust committee-based approaches are computationally prohibitive in multi-turn interactions. MAS-Shield introduces a coarse-to-fine filtering pipeline to balance these concerns.

## [Small Language Models Enable Cost-Effective NL2KQL Threat Hunting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.06660) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2512.06660v3) proposes a three-knob framework for translating natural language queries into Kusto Query Language (KQL) using small language models (SLMs), evaluating nine models on Microsoft's NL2KQL Defender Evaluation dataset. The two-stage architecture pairing an SLM drafter with a low-cost LLM judge achieves 0.987 syntax and 0.906 schema-valid accuracy at USD 0.213 for 230 queries, a 9.5-14x cost reduction versus GPT-4o and GPT-5 baselines. Writing correct KQL requires specialized expertise that bottlenecks security operations centers, so enabling accurate, low-cost natural language querying could help security teams scale threat hunting without hiring scarce KQL specialists. The results suggest SLMs can match or exceed large LLM accuracy at a fraction of the cost, making AI-assisted security operations more practical for budget-constrained teams. The three-knob framework spans prompting, fine-tuning, and architecture: error-aware prompting targets common parser failures with mined tips, while LoRA fine-tuning with rationale distillation produced an informative negative result—neither variant surpassed targeted prompting. The two-stage SLM drafter plus LLM judge also generalized to independently authored queries (0.964 syntax, 0.831 schema-valid), and the only baselines within 0.05 schema-valid were NL2KQL+GPT-4o (0.878) and NL2KQL+GPT-5 (0.861), costing USD 2.998 and USD 2.018 respectively.

Small language models (SLMs) are compact models trained to perform specific tasks with fewer resources than large language models (LLMs), making them attractive for cost-sensitive, latency-sensitive applications. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts large models on standard GPUs with aggressive memory savings.

## [FedVideoMAE: Federated Video Moderation with Differential Privacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18809) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released FedVideoMAE, a federated learning framework that adapts a frozen VideoMAE backbone using lightweight LoRA and prompt parameters for violence detection in short-form videos. Each training round combines self-supervised masked video reconstruction with client-side differential privacy and pairwise masked secure aggregation, exchanging 5,518,848 trainable parameters instead of the full 156,371,328-parameter model state (a 28.3x payload reduction). This work addresses a practical privacy-sensitive problem: moderating short-form videos on edge devices without sending raw clips to a central server, which reduces exposure, bandwidth, and latency. It provides concrete privacy-utility trade-off numbers that could inform the design of privacy-preserving ML systems for content moderation and edge computing. On RWF-2000, FedVideoMAE reaches 77.25% test accuracy without differential privacy or secure aggregation, while accuracy under DP+SA remains in the 65.25-66.00% range; transfer experiments on RLVS and binary UCF-Crime show similar behavior. Violence labels are held out from federation and used only for downstream evaluation, separating private representation learning from supervised assessment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: VideoMAE is a self-supervised video pre-training method that uses masked autoencoders and a plain ViT backbone to learn from unlabeled video. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that freezes pre-trained weights and injects small trainable rank decomposition matrices, greatly reducing trainable parameters. Federated learning trains models across decentralized devices without sharing raw data, but unprotected model updates can still leak information, so differential privacy and secure aggregation are used to protect updates during aggregation.

## [VLM-CAD: Neuro-Symbolic Agent Workflow for Analog Circuit Sizing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced VLM-CAD, a collaborative agent design workflow that combines a neuro-symbolic structural parsing module called Image2Net with an explainable trust region Bayesian optimization method called ExTuRBO to improve Vision Language Model reasoning for analog circuit sizing. On 12 sizing tasks spanning six circuits and four technology platforms, VLM-CAD achieved a pooled Strict Pass@1 of 23.3% and a Relaxed Pass@1 of 91.7% while producing sensitivity evidence for final design reports. Analog circuit sizing is a notoriously slow, expert-driven bottleneck in chip design, and this work shows that grounding VLMs in deterministic symbolic representations can overcome their spatial blindness and logical hallucinations on dense engineering schematics. If the approach generalizes, it could meaningfully accelerate AI-assisted analog design automation and reduce reliance on scarce expert designers. Image2Net transforms raw schematic pixels into explicit topological graphs and structured JSON representations to anchor VLM interpretation in deterministic facts, while ExTuRBO uses agent-generated semantic seeds to warm-start local searches and Automatic Relevance Determination to supply sensitivity evidence. The headline Strict Pass@1 of 23.3% remains modest, indicating that fully correct first-shot sizing is still difficult even with the neuro-symbolic scaffolding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Language Models can read images and text together but often misread densely structured engineering content such as analog circuit schematics, producing spatial errors and fabricated reasoning. Neuro-symbolic AI addresses this by pairing neural perception with symbolic, rule-based representations that are verifiable, while Bayesian optimization is a standard sample-efficient method for tuning expensive black-box design parameters. Trust region Bayesian optimization (TuRBO) improves on global Bayesian optimization by focusing local searches within dynamically resized regions, which is well suited to high-dimensional analog sizing problems.

## [KinshipQA Benchmark Exposes LLM Cultural Kinship Reasoning Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07794) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced KinshipQA, a procedurally-generated benchmark covering seven anthropologically-documented kinship systems (Eskimo, Sudanese, Hawaiian, Iroquois, Dravidian, Crow, Omaha) and up to six reasoning hops, with a tunable simulator horizon that eliminates exact-instance pretraining overlap. Evaluating six LLMs, they found a 40.9% accuracy drop when reasoning shifts from biological multi-hop to culturally-marked classification on the five non-descriptive systems, with performance falling to 10.6% at 5-6 hops. This work exposes a significant limitation in LLMs' compositional reasoning across diverse cultural kinship systems, showing that models rely on familiar English surface forms rather than genuine rule application. The findings highlight that current benchmarks like CLUTRR, which cover only the Eskimo system, may overestimate LLM reasoning abilities, and that cultural override rules remain a major blind spot for even advanced models. The accuracy drop persists under chain-of-thought and few-shot prompting, and is largest for the two skewing systems (Crow, Omaha). Under identical rule access, humans reach 89.0% versus 50.7% for LLMs, and a fictional-rule control swapping system labels for invented strings raises accuracy by 6.1%, implicating familiar English surface forms.

**Background**: Kinship systems are anthropological classifications of family relationships, with six major types identified by Lewis H. The Eskimo system, which underlies English kinship terms, emphasizes the nuclear family, while Crow and Omaha are skewing systems with distinct cross-generational rules. Multi-hop reasoning requires composing multiple relational steps, making kinship a natural testbed for evaluating LLM compositionality beyond simple pattern matching.

A new arXiv paper introduces CoMa, a study on contextual building massing generation using vision-language models (VLMs), built on a dataset of 12,845 Melbourne massings with parcel contours, structured 3D geometry, neighboring buildings, top-down views, and multi-view 3D context images. Using Qwen3-VL models, the authors compare no-context, unimodal-context, and multimodal-context training regimes, and introduce a learned contextual relevance metric to evaluate whether generated massings are morphologically compatible with their surroundings. The public dataset and the learned relevance metric could become useful benchmarks for future research at the intersection of generative design and multimodal learning. The results show that model size strongly affects generation quality, multimodal training improves the use of individual modalities, and multimodal inference yields a stronger contextual signal than isolated context inputs. The task is inherently multimodal because the target output must remain structured and editable while context can be represented as vector geometry, map imagery, or 3D views.

## [Why β1=β2 Is Dynamically Special in Adam: A Lag-Term Explanation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21739) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2601.21739v3) identifies a concrete mechanism explaining why tying Adam's momentum parameters β1=β2 is dynamically special. In the continuous-time limit, each normalized-update coordinate decomposes into a sign component, a magnitude-lag term proportional to the difference between the two memory times, and additional transition, curvature, and nonlinear ratio terms; the lag channel vanishes exactly when β1=β2. Adam has been the core optimizer for large-scale deep learning training for nearly a decade, yet the role of its two momentum parameters has remained poorly understood. This work provides a mechanistic account that could inform hyperparameter choices and training practices, especially given recent findings that tying β1=β2 can preserve strong performance while collapsing two memory scales into one. The paper performs a full-history discrete decomposition on real training gradients, showing that tied updates are sign-dominated while the lag term becomes substantial off the diagonal and leaves a comparatively small residual. Across six vision and language tasks, tied configurations also typically exhibit smoother update-norm trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Adam (Adaptive Moment Estimation) is an optimizer that combines momentum and adaptive scaling, using two exponential moving averages: β1 controls the decay rate of the first moment (gradient mean), and β2 controls the decay rate of the second moment (squared gradient). Default values are typically β1=0.9 and β2=0.999, meaning the two memory scales differ by orders of magnitude. Recent work has shown that setting β1=β2 can preserve Adam's performance despite collapsing these two memory scales into one, motivating the question of what becomes dynamically special when the memories are tied.

## [ELBO Likelihood Estimation Is Key to RL for Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04663) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.04663) systematically disentangles the reinforcement learning design space for diffusion models into three factors: policy-gradient objectives, likelihood estimators, and rollout sampling schemes. The authors find that an ELBO-based likelihood estimator computed only from the final generated sample is the dominant factor for effective, efficient, and stable RL optimization, outweighing the choice of policy-gradient loss. Using SD 3.5 Medium, their method improves the GenEval score from 0.24 to 0.95 in 90 GPU hours, which is 4.6× more efficient than FlowGRPO and 2× more efficient than the state-of-the-art method without reward hacking. This insight could reshape how researchers approach RL fine-tuning of diffusion and flow models for text-to-image generation, shifting focus from crafting new policy-gradient losses to better likelihood estimation. It offers a more efficient and stable training recipe that may influence future work in generative model alignment and reward optimization. The study validates findings across multiple reward benchmarks using SD 3.5 Medium, showing consistent trends. The ELBO-based estimator is computed only from the final generated sample, and the method achieves 4.6× efficiency over FlowGRPO and 2× over SOTA without reward hacking.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising random noise, but their likelihoods are intractable, making it difficult to apply standard policy-gradient reinforcement learning methods that require likelihood estimates. The evidence lower bound (ELBO) is a common variational lower bound on the log-likelihood used in variational autoencoders and diffusion models. Existing RL approaches for diffusion models often borrow objectives from large language models and use ad hoc likelihood estimators without thoroughly studying their impact.

## [Phase Attacks Reveal Adversarial Robustness Gaps in Complex-Valued Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06577) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.06577v2) introduces Phase Attacks, a class of adversarial perturbations that specifically target the phase component of complex-valued inputs, and derives complex-valued versions of common adversarial attacks. Experiments show that Phase Attacks degrade model performance more than equally strong regular attacks that perturb both phase and magnitude, and that CVNNs can be more robust than RVNNs in some scenarios. As CVNNs gain popularity in safety-critical domains such as audio, radar, and communications, understanding their worst-case behavior under adversarial perturbations becomes essential for deployment. The finding that phase information is a particularly fragile attack surface could reshape how robustness is evaluated and defended in complex-valued models. The paper derives complex-valued analogues of standard adversarial attacks and compares them against the proposed Phase Attacks, showing that phase-only perturbations are more damaging than equally strong attacks that modify both phase and magnitude. It also reports that CVNNs outperform RVNNs in robustness under certain conditions, though both remain highly susceptible to phase changes.

Adversarial attacks are small, worst-case input perturbations designed to fool a model, and they have been a major research focus since 2014 when gradient-based attacks were shown to fool deep networks. Phase information is often neglected in real-valued networks but is a core part of CVNN representations, making it a natural target for adversarial study.

## [FASCL: Future-Aligned Soft Contrastive Learning for Asset Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10711) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2602.10711) proposes Future-Aligned Soft Contrastive Learning (FASCL), a representation learning framework that retrieves financial assets based on predicted future return correlation rather than historical price patterns or sector classifications. Experiments on 5,631 US-listed securities against 14 baselines show FASCL achieves the best future return correlation at every retrieval depth, the best rank information coefficient at every depth and horizon, and the highest gross Sharpe ratio in a spread trading backtest at every basket size. This work challenges the backward-looking similarity assumption that dominates quantitative asset retrieval, potentially improving portfolio construction, risk management, and statistical arbitrage by grouping assets that are likely to co-move in the future. It also introduces a standardized evaluation protocol for future-aligned cross-sectional similarity, which could become a benchmark for subsequent research in financial machine learning. FASCL uses a soft contrastive loss where pairwise future return correlations serve as continuous supervision targets, rather than binary positive/negative pairs. The evaluation protocol directly measures whether retrieved assets share similar future trajectories, and the code is publicly available on GitHub at https://github.com/HyeongminLEE/fascl.

**Background**: Asset retrieval in quantitative finance traditionally relies on historical price patterns or sector labels to find similar securities, but such backward-looking criteria cannot guarantee future co-movement. Contrastive learning is a representation learning technique that trains models to pull similar samples together and push dissimilar ones apart in an embedding space, and soft contrastive learning extends this by using continuous similarity targets instead of hard binary labels. FASCL applies this idea to finance by aligning asset embeddings with future return correlations, enabling retrieval that is explicitly optimized for future behavior.

## [TTSR: Test-Time Self-Evolving via Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03297) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TTSR is a test-time training framework in which a single pretrained LLM alternates between a Student role that solves test questions and updates, and a Teacher role that analyzes failed trajectories and synthesizes targeted variant questions near the Student's capability frontier. It also maintains a cross-iteration weakness memory and compiles persistent weaknesses into a lightweight strategy note prepended to later Student inputs, yielding consistent gains on challenging mathematical reasoning benchmarks, strong cross-backbone generalization, and transfer to general-domain reasoning tasks. Test-time training lets LLMs adapt during inference using only unlabeled test inputs, but existing methods struggle on hard reasoning tasks because self-generated pseudo-labels are noisy and exploration is inefficient. TTSR's reflect-then-synthesize paradigm directly diagnoses why prior attempts fail and turns that diagnosis into targeted practice, which could make inference-time self-improvement more sample-efficient and more reliable for researchers working on LLM reasoning. TTSR targets two bottlenecks of existing test-time training: the lack of learnable samples caused by noisy pseudo-labels and unstable rewards on difficult questions, and inefficient exploration that relies on repeatedly sampling many rollouts without explicit failure diagnosis. Its strategy note is designed to gradually fade as weaknesses are resolved, and the reported results cover mathematical reasoning benchmarks, cross-backbone generalization, and transfer to general-domain reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time training (TTT) refers to adapting a large language model during inference rather than relying solely on weights fixed after pretraining, typically using only unlabeled test inputs. Self-evolving LLM methods aim to let models improve themselves through internal reflection, autonomous data engineering, and continual self-updating, and prior work such as SELF has used language feedback for self-reflection. TTSR combines these two lines of work by having one model play both Student and Teacher roles, generating its own training signal at test time without any labeled data.

## [MAPLE Uses Metadata to Fix Private Evolution's Initialization Bottleneck](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19258) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.19258v3) introduces Metadata Augmented Private Language Evolution (MAPLE), which extracts differentially private (DP) tabular metadata and uses in-context learning to ground the initial synthetic distribution in the target domain. Evaluations on domain-specific text generation show MAPLE achieves a strictly better privacy-utility trade-off, converges significantly faster, and sharply reduces API costs compared to baseline Private Evolution (PE) methods. DP fine-tuning of LLMs demands massive compute and full model access, which excludes proprietary APIs for most users; generating DP synthetic data via APIs is a practical workaround, and MAPLE makes that workaround viable for highly specialized domains where PE previously failed. This could broaden access to privacy-preserving synthetic data generation for users who cannot fine-tune models themselves. MAPLE's key mechanism is extracting DP tabular metadata and injecting it through in-context learning to initialize the synthetic distribution, directly addressing PE's heavy reliance on initialization when private data falls outside the foundation model's pre-training priors. The paper reports faster convergence and reduced API call costs, though it remains a specialized arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion at this time.

**Background**: Differential privacy (DP) is a formal guarantee that a computation's output reveals little about any single individual's data. Private Evolution (PE) is an API-based framework that iteratively prompts a foundation model to generate synthetic data resembling a private dataset while satisfying DP. However, PE's success depends heavily on initialization: if the private data distribution lies far outside the model's pre-training priors—common in specialized domains—PE converges poorly and wastes API calls.

## [Domain Elastic Transform: Grid-Free Bayesian Registration for High-Dimensional Scientific Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21235) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Domain Elastic Transform (DET), a grid-free probabilistic framework that jointly aligns geometry and function on irregular domains, avoiding voxelization or binning. Evaluated on MERFISH mouse-brain slices and Stereo-seq mouse-embryo atlases, DET achieved the strongest spatial overlap and topology on a 90-case MERFISH benchmark with severe perturbations and no prior initialization. This work addresses a key limitation in scientific data analysis such as spatial transcriptomics, where high-dimensional vector-valued functions like gene expression are defined on irregular sparse manifolds. By complementing point-set, image-based, and optimal-transport approaches, DET could enable more accurate alignment of emerging scientific data without sacrificing single-cell resolution or functional signals. DET models domain deformation as elastic motion guided by a joint spatial-functional likelihood within a generalized Bayesian formulation, and is fully unsupervised and scalable through registration on sampled points followed by displacement interpolation. The implementation is available at https://github.com/ohirose/bcpd since March 2025.

**Background**: Nonrigid registration is conventionally divided into point set registration, which aligns sparse geometries, and image registration, which aligns continuous intensity fields on regular grids. Spatial transcriptomics captures the positional context of gene expression within intact tissue, producing high-dimensional data on irregular domains. Bayesian inference provides a statistical framework for updating probabilities as new evidence becomes available, which DET uses to model deformation.

## [Perplexity Hides Generation Gaps in Distilled Hybrid Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26556) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2603.26556v3) shows that a 7B distilled model matching its teacher within 0.2 percentage points under log-likelihood scoring falls behind by 20.8 percentage points when required to generate answers autoregressively. The authors introduce GenDistill, a multi-stage distillation pipeline that converts a pretrained Transformer into an efficient Hybrid Kimi Delta Attention (Hybrid-KDA) student, and use it to ablate six design axes on Qwen3-0.6B under both log-likelihood and generation-based evaluation. The finding challenges a widespread evaluation practice in distillation research, where multiple-choice benchmarks scored by log-likelihood can mask severe generation degradation and even reverse the ranking of design choices. This matters for anyone deploying distilled or hybrid models for real inference, since conclusions drawn from perplexity-only evaluation may be misleading and lead to suboptimal architecture and training decisions. Among the six ablated axes, dataset selection, completion-only masking, and freezing attention layers during post-training have the largest impact on generation quality. The best recipe retains 86-90% of teacher accuracy on knowledge benchmarks while cutting KV cache memory by up to 75% and improving time-to-first-token by 2-4x at 128K-token contexts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large pretrained model (the teacher) into a smaller or more efficient model (the student) so it can run on cheaper hardware. Hybrid sequence models combine linear attention mechanisms, such as Kimi Delta Attention (KDA), with periodic full attention layers—typically in a 3:1 ratio—to reduce memory and KV-cache usage during long-sequence generation. Log-likelihood scoring ranks candidate answers by the probability the model assigns to them, whereas autoregressive generation requires the model to produce each token sequentially, which is a stricter and more realistic test of generation quality.

## [StarVLA-α: A Minimalist Baseline for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11757) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced StarVLA-α, a deliberately simplified Vision-Language-Action (VLA) baseline that systematically re-evaluates key design axes such as action modeling strategies, robot-specific pretraining, and interface engineering. Trained under a unified multi-benchmark setup on LIBERO, SimplerEnv, RoboTwin, and RoboCasa, the same simple model remains highly competitive, and a single generalist model outperforms π0.5 by 20% on the public real-world RoboChallenge benchmark. The VLA field is highly fragmented, with approaches differing widely in architecture, training data, and engineering tricks, which makes it hard to tell what actually drives performance. By showing that a strong VLM backbone plus minimal design is already sufficient, StarVLA-α offers the community a clean, reproducible starting point and could steer future research away from unnecessary complexity. The baseline deliberately minimizes architectural and pipeline complexity to reduce experimental confounders, and it is evaluated across four simulation benchmarks plus the real-world RoboChallenge. The authors state that code will be released at https://github.com/starVLA/starVLA, though the paper is a v2 arXiv preprint and the results have not necessarily been peer-reviewed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take an image of the robot's surroundings plus a text instruction and directly output low-level robot actions. They are typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model (VLM) on large datasets pairing visual observations and language with robot trajectories, a paradigm pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in 2023. Benchmarks like LIBERO (lifelong robot learning), SimplerEnv (simulated replicas of real-world setups), RoboTwin, and RoboCasa are standard evaluation suites for such manipulation policies.

## [NEO Learns to Theorize the World from Raw Observation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03413) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (arXiv:2605.03413v3) introduces Learning-to-Theorize, a paradigm in which the Neural Theorizer (NEO) induces explicit explanatory theories as latent, executable programs from raw non-textual observations. NEO represents each theory as a compositional program in a learned Language of Thought, executed through a shared transition model, enabling explanation-driven generalization. The work challenges the dominant view that world models should be judged mainly by future prediction accuracy, proposing instead that understanding means constructing explicit theories. If validated, this could influence research in AI world modeling, program induction, and cognitive science by offering a theory-building alternative to purely predictive architectures. NEO is a probabilistic neural model that infers latent executable programs from paired observations, with learned primitives that can be systematically recombined to explain novel phenomena. As an arXiv preprint, it has not yet undergone peer review, and its practical scalability and broader empirical validation remain open questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Contemporary world models typically treat understanding as accurate future prediction in latent or observation space. Developmental cognitive science suggests instead that humans build internal theories of how the world works, even before acquiring mature language. The Language of Thought hypothesis, proposed by philosopher Jerry Fodor, holds that thinking occurs in an internal, compositional, language-like representational system. Program induction, meanwhile, refers to learning programs or algorithms from incomplete specifications, and NEO combines these ideas by inducing latent programs as explicit theories.

## [Clin-JEPA Co-Trains LLM Encoder and Predictor for EHR Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10840) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Clin-JEPA introduces a five-phase curriculum that stably co-trains an LLM encoder with a latent trajectory predictor on MIMIC-IV, addressing the rollout degradation that occurs when the encoder is frozen as in V-JEPA 2-AC. Under 48-hour autoregressive rollout, the co-trained predictor degrades only ×1.06 versus ×1.23–1.36 for two-stage designs, and the co-trained encoder separates deteriorating from stable patients with Cohen's d=1.59 versus ≤0.50 for two-stage encoders. This is the first exploration of bringing JEPA-style latent world models to EHR patient trajectories, potentially enabling a single set of embeddings to serve many clinical prediction tasks without per-task fine-tuning. If validated, such latent patient simulators could support planning and risk prediction in clinical AI, reducing reliance on feature engineering and vocabulary harmonisation. The framework uses an LLM as the encoder that reads hourly records as text, avoiding feature engineering and vocabulary harmonisation, but naïve co-training is unstable because the untrained predictor drags the encoder toward collapse and the predictor's rollout diverges as its target space moves. One set of embeddings serves 34 downstream tasks across three benchmarks, outperforming strong per-task tuned baselines and a pretrained EHR foundation model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Joint-embedding predictive architectures (JEPA), such as those proposed by Yann LeCun, learn representations by predicting in latent space rather than reconstructing raw inputs, and retaining an action-conditioned predictor at inference turns them into latent world models. V-JEPA 2-AC demonstrated this for robotic planning by post-training a latent action-conditioned world model on robot videos. Clin-JEPA adapts this idea to electronic health records, where patient trajectories are hourly clinical records that a model must simulate in latent space.

## [EfficientTDMPC Improves TD-MPC Sample Efficiency in Continuous Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16692) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EfficientTDMPC introduces three improvements to the TD-MPC family of model-based RL algorithms: an aggregate multi-horizon planning objective that averages value estimates across different rollout depths, ensembles for state-action value estimation in value-equivalent/MuZero-style methods, and pessimistic reanalyze that penalizes uncertain return estimates when creating policy targets. The authors report that it achieves new state-of-the-art sample efficiency on both HumanoidBench and the DeepMind Control Suite. Sample efficiency is a central bottleneck for applying reinforcement learning to real-world continuous control tasks such as robotics, where each interaction with the environment is costly. By reducing estimation error from learned models and value networks, EfficientTDMPC offers an incremental but immediately useful advance for RL researchers and practitioners working on model-based control. The three contributions target different sources of error: the aggregate multi-horizon objective mitigates value estimation bias across rollout depths, the value ensembles reduce variance in state-action value estimates, and pessimistic reanalyze curbs overestimation when constructing policy targets. Evaluation is limited to simulated benchmarks (HumanoidBench and DeepMind Control Suite), so real-robot transfer remains untested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: TD-MPC is a model-based reinforcement learning algorithm that performs local trajectory optimization in the latent space of a learned implicit (decoder-free) world model, combining short-horizon model predictive control (MPC) with long-horizon temporal difference learning. MuZero-style value-equivalent methods learn a dynamics model whose predictions are only required to be accurate with respect to value, rather than reconstructing observations. In these approaches, both the learned model and the value network introduce errors that can degrade planning quality, which is the problem EfficientTDMPC aims to address.

## [Reinforcement Learning Achieves Hard Assortativity Constraints in Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23285) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.23285v2) presents a reinforcement learning framework that drives degree-preserving rewirings to satisfy a prescribed assortativity in every generated graph, replacing the entropically dominated Metropolis-Hastings random walk with directed transport. The learned policy reduces generation cost by at least an order of magnitude while retaining over 98% configurational diversity, and it generalizes across graph sizes and topologies without retraining. This work establishes reinforcement learning as a practical paradigm for hard-constrained graph generation, enabling quantitative isolation of secondary observables such as the clustering coefficient. It could significantly impact network science and generative modeling by making it feasible to generate large ensembles of graphs with precisely controlled structural properties. The method enforces hard constraints with prescribed precision in every realization, unlike canonical ensembles that impose constraints only in expectation (soft constraints). It generalizes across graph sizes and topologies without retraining, and the authors demonstrate its ability to isolate secondary observables like the clustering coefficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Assortativity measures the preference for nodes in a network to connect to others with similar degree, and it is a key structural property in network science. Degree-preserving rewiring is a standard technique that randomly swaps edges while keeping each node's degree unchanged, often used to randomize graphs. Metropolis-Hastings is a Markov chain Monte Carlo method for sampling from complex distributions, but it can be inefficient for hard-constrained graph generation due to entropic effects.

## [Batch Normalization Amplifies Memorization and Privacy Risks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24420) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.24420v2) shows that Batch Normalization (BN) substantially increases memorization of atypical or outlier training samples and makes models significantly more susceptible to membership inference attacks (MIA). The authors demonstrate this across multiple datasets and architectures using three complementary approaches: unintended memorization of out-of-distribution samples, per-sample influence, and MIA susceptibility. This finding reveals an underappreciated privacy risk in a technique that is almost universally used to speed up and stabilize deep network training, meaning many deployed models may leak more information about rare or sensitive training examples than previously assumed. It has direct implications for privacy auditing, regulation, and the design of normalization layers in safety-critical or privacy-sensitive applications. The paper complements its empirical results with a mechanistic analysis under the exact BN backward pass, showing that BN amplifies the per-step margin growth of outlier samples during training. The effect is consistent across datasets and architectures, though the study focuses on empirical and theoretical analysis rather than proposing a specific mitigation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Batch Normalization is a widely adopted technique that normalizes layer activations during training, re-centering them around zero and rescaling them to a standard size to make training faster and more stable. Memorization refers to a model's tendency to fit idiosyncratic properties of individual training examples, while membership inference attacks (MIAs) are privacy attacks that determine whether a specific record was part of a model's training set. Prior work has studied memorization and MIAs separately, but the link between normalization layers and these privacy risks had remained largely unexplored.

## [Comparing Legal Formalizations by Their Behavioral Divergences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25186) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.25186v2) introduces a method that compares different formalizations of the same legal provision by matching them at the node level, deriving a shared interface, and using a SAT solver to enumerate edge cases where any two formalizations disagree. Applied to formalizations of ten EU provisions generated by nine frontier LLMs, the method finds that behavioral divergence is essentially uncorrelated with structural agreement, and the verbalized edge cases reveal qualitatively distinct types of disagreement, including ones mirroring genuine controversies in legal commentary. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in AI and law: as LLMs make it tempting to generate legal formalizations directly from statutory text, implicit interpretive choices become hard to anticipate. By providing a systematic way to surface and verbalize behavioral divergences, the method could improve trust in automated legal reasoning, help legal experts audit LLM-generated formalizations, and inform the design of safer legal AI systems. The method matches formalizations at the node level, derives a shared interface for each pair, and uses a SAT solver to enumerate edge cases on which the two disagree; selected cases are then verbalized into concrete factual scenarios for legal expert review. The study covers ten EU provisions and nine frontier LLMs, and notably finds that structural agreement between formalizations does not predict behavioral agreement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Legal formalization is the translation of legal texts into machine-readable logical representations that preserve legal meaning, and it is a necessary first step for automated legal rule-based reasoning. SAT solvers are programs that determine whether a Boolean formula has a satisfying assignment, and they are widely used in software verification, program analysis, and artificial intelligence. Recent advances in LLMs have made it feasible to generate such formalizations directly from statutory text, but any formalization embeds interpretive choices whose consequences are difficult to foresee.

## [BTS-CAFE Tackles Stethoscope-Induced Shortcuts in Federated Respiratory Sound AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29862) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2605.29862v2) introduces BTS-CAFE, a federated domain generalization framework for respiratory sound classification that combines causality-inspired device-style interventions, counterfactual metadata augmentation, and gradient alignment. Built on the BTS with CLAP multimodal language-audio pretraining model, it improves the out-of-distribution ICBHI Score by 3.69 points on average across five held-out stethoscope devices compared to its backbone. Multi-site deployment of AI respiratory sound classifiers is currently blocked by variability between stethoscopes, so a method that generalizes to unseen devices without sharing patient data could make automated pulmonary disease screening more practical across hospitals. The work also shows how causality-inspired techniques can address shortcut learning in federated medical audio, an area where conventional domain generalization has struggled. The authors' empirical analysis finds that stethoscope-induced style and disease-relevant content are partially entangled, making deterministic style removal unreliable, which motivates the constrained intervention design. BTS-CAFE is evaluated on the ICBHI and SPRSound datasets using simulated device leave-out protocols, and it outperforms conventional data augmentation and federated learning baselines.

**Background**: Federated learning lets multiple hospitals train a shared model without exchanging raw patient data, while domain generalization aims to make that model work on data from devices or sites it never saw during training; combining the two is called federated domain generalization. Respiratory sound classification uses audio recordings of breathing to detect pulmonary diseases, but different stethoscope models impart distinct acoustic styles that can act as shortcuts, leading models to learn device identity instead of disease signals. CLAP is a multimodal model that links audio and natural-language descriptions through contrastive learning, and BTS is the backbone that BTS-CAFE builds upon.

## [AnyAudio-Judge: Dynamic Rubric Benchmark for Audio Instruction Following](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03116) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a dynamic rubric-based evaluation paradigm that adaptively decomposes complex audio captions into a variable number of independent, verifiable binary rubric items, alongside the AnyAudio-Judge Bench, a bilingual benchmark of 7,920 curated samples across speech, sound, music, and mixed domains with hard negatives. It also releases a 105K-sample Chain-of-Thought corpus and trains the AnyAudio-Judge model (built on Qwen2.5-Omni-7B) using SFT and GRPO to align reasoning with rubric-based scoring. Current automated evaluation of instruction-guided audio generation relies on holistic scoring from general-purpose LLMs, which struggle to decouple complex instructions, lack interpretability, and miss fine-grained attribute mismatches. This work provides a more interpretable and fine-grained evaluation and reward signal, potentially improving both benchmarking and downstream reinforcement learning for audio generation. The benchmark contains 7,920 bilingual samples across four audio domains with deliberately constructed hard negatives, and the evaluator is trained on a 105K-sample CoT corpus using a pipeline combining SFT and GRPO. Experiments show AnyAudio-Judge improves zero-shot alignment detection over state-of-the-art baselines and yields precise, interpretable reward signals that enhance instruction alignment in downstream RL for audio generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Instruction-guided audio generation aims to produce speech, sound, or music that follows a natural-language instruction, and evaluating how well the output matches the instruction is a core challenge. Many current evaluators use large language models to give a single holistic score, which is hard to interpret and can miss specific mismatches. Rubric-based evaluation instead breaks an instruction into smaller verifiable checks, and hard negatives are deliberately wrong samples designed to test whether a model truly relies on acoustic evidence rather than linguistic plausibility.

## [Layer-Wise KV Cache Eviction Routing Explored](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15157) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.15157v2) systematically explores whether KV cache eviction methods should vary across Transformer layers, composing existing eviction methods into heterogeneous layer-wise routes guided by simple offline profiles. On LongBench, these heterogeneous routes outperform uniform policies on a majority of tasks at the same cache budget, and the same fixed route beats the best of nine standalone baselines across all five tested cache budgets. This work reframes eviction-method identity from a model-level choice to a layer-wise design dimension, suggesting that existing KV cache compression methods may be leaving performance on the table by applying a single retention rule throughout a model. If validated, layer-wise routing could offer a low-cost way to improve long-context LLM inference efficiency without changing the underlying model. The study uses simple offline profiles to construct fixed routes and evaluates how route quality varies with method placement and cache budget; even when the number of methods is held fixed, the profile-guided placement ranks second among 100 evaluated assignments, showing that routing quality depends strongly on where methods are placed. The abstract does not report absolute scores or latency overheads, and the evaluation is limited to LongBench.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: KV cache stores key and value tensors for previously processed tokens during autoregressive LLM generation, and its memory footprint grows linearly with sequence length, making it a major bottleneck for long-context inference. KV cache eviction methods reduce this footprint by discarding less important cached tokens according to a retention rule, but most approaches apply a single rule family uniformly across all Transformer layers. Transformer layers are known to differ in attention behavior and sensitivity to compression, which motivates the question of whether eviction rules should also differ by layer. LongBench is a widely used benchmark for evaluating long-context understanding and reasoning across multiple real-world tasks.

A new arXiv preprint (2606.22790v4) presents a compression framework that jointly parametrizes Whisper ASR deployment along six dimensions—model size, temporal resolution, encoder token stride, low-rank adaptation capacity, weight precision, and sparsity pattern—and optimizes them with NSGA-III against word error rate (WER), inference FLOPs, and memory footprint. Across 50 of 1,680 candidate configurations, the authors identify compression combinations that dominate naive single-axis scaling, while finding that 1:4 structured sparsity fails to recover acceptable accuracy under tested recovery budgets. As large ASR models like Whisper must run on hardware with widely varying memory and speed constraints, this work offers a principled multi-objective recipe for practitioners to pick deployment configurations rather than tuning one axis at a time. The finding that joint optimization beats single-axis scaling could influence how efficient ASR inference is engineered in production. The framework reports measured WER and resident memory, uses analytical EffFLOPs as a search-time compute surrogate, and separately validates representative inference configurations with measured real-time factor (RTF). A notable negative result is that 1:4 structured sparsity did not recover acceptable accuracy within the tested recovery budgets.

NSGA-III is a many-objective evolutionary algorithm that uses reference directions to find a diverse set of non-dominated solutions across several objectives. ## [Exact-Match Recall Misleads in Tool-Use Agent Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23937) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.23937) shows that exact-match retrieval recall can mislead when evaluating retrievers for pre-action policy classification in long-horizon tool-use agents. Using Qwen2.5-3B/7B classifiers on τ-bench, the authors find that although the exact governing rule is retrieved at rank 1 for only 7% of airline states, retrieved assertions achieve macro-F1 0.58 versus 0.60 for the gold rule (Δ=-0.02, 95% CI [-0.23, +0.21]), while random non-gold and no-assertion controls score only 0.32 and 0.21. This finding challenges the common assumption that exact-match retrieval recall is a reliable proxy for downstream policy utility, suggesting that retrieval should be evaluated inside the classification loop rather than by exact-match recall alone. It has direct implications for how retrieval-augmented agents and their evaluation metrics are designed in long-horizon tool-use settings. The study uses Qwen2.5-3B and 7B classifiers on τ-bench, where a compact structured state improves macro-F1 over raw trajectories by 0.20 after tuning at 3B. The authors note that the confidence interval remains too wide to establish non-inferiority, and the same qualitative pattern appears with a second retriever and at 7B, though results vary across fine-tuning configurations.

## [Mean-Expansion Layer Speeds Up Q-Learning by Sharing Values Across Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29806) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.29806v3) introduces the mean-expansion layer, a parameter-free architectural addition that accelerates action-value learning in Q-learning by sharing values across actions within a state and learning a lower-norm representation of action values. Applied to deep Q-networks and implicit quantile networks, it improves aggregate performance across 57 Atari 2600 games while increasing action gaps and dramatically reducing value overestimation. Action-value learning is central to reinforcement learning, but it is often slow because values must move from near-zero initialization to potentially large true values, and each state-action pair is updated independently. This work offers a simple, algorithm-agnostic modification that could make value-based deep RL training faster and more stable, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The mean-expansion layer is parameter-free and can be added to Q-network architectures without altering the underlying algorithm, changing the problem from directly learning potentially large action-values to learning a lower-norm representation. Experiments show improved aggregate performance across 57 Atari 2600 games, increased action gaps, and dramatically reduced value overestimation, though the paper does not claim a paradigm-shifting breakthrough.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Q-learning is a model-free reinforcement learning algorithm in which an agent learns the value of taking actions in states through trial and error, typically using a Q-table or, in deep Q-networks (DQNs), a neural network to approximate action values. Deep Q-networks combine Q-learning with deep neural networks to handle high-dimensional inputs such as Atari game frames. Implicit quantile networks are a distributional RL method that learns a distribution over returns rather than a single expected value. The mean-expansion layer is a neural network component that exposes mean structure to improve representation and optimization.

## [WorldRoamBench: A New Benchmark for Long-Horizon Stability of Interactive World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31672) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced WorldRoamBench, an open-world benchmark that evaluates the long-horizon stability of interactive world models (IWMs) across four dimensions: action, vision, physics, and memory. It contains over 600 test cases spanning Nature, Urban, and Indoor scenes in first- and third-person views with 10–60 seconds of continuous WASD interaction, and evaluating 10+ open- and closed-source models shows none reliably satisfies all dimensions. Existing benchmarks only measure action following at the trajectory level and ignore memory and interaction physics, so WorldRoamBench fills a key evaluation gap for researchers in AI/ML and robotics. Its finding that even the best model achieves only moderate scores highlights how far interactive world models remain from being stable, physically grounded, and deployable in real-world applications. The benchmark introduces tailored metrics for each dimension: a per-frame action metric that bypasses cross-model semantic scale disparity, a segment-based drift metric that captures non-monotonic mid-sequence collapse, a controllability-gated physics evaluation over mechanics, optics, and 3D consistency, and an action-decoupled memory protocol using transition-localized 3D point-cloud reconstruction and tracking-plus-VLM reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Interactive world models (IWMs) are AI systems that continually generate video by responding to a user's actions, enabling open-ended generation rather than passive video playback. Long-horizon stability refers to whether such a model maintains consistent behavior over extended multi-step interactions, which is essential for real-world deployment. Vision-language models (VLMs) are AI systems that jointly interpret images and text, and WorldRoamBench uses them for subject-memory reasoning.

## [Prompt-Driven Exploration Uses VLMs to Guide RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08837) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2607.08837v3) introduces Prompt-Driven Exploration (PDE), a method where a vision-language model watches rollout videos, diagnoses how the policy behaved, and rewrites the natural language prompt to elicit better behavior. Across manipulation and reasoning tasks, PDE enables reinforcement learning to learn successful policies even from zero-reward starts and improves sample efficiency. Standard exploration methods only add action noise, which produces rollouts close to the original and cannot escape a weak policy; PDE instead induces global behavioral changes by modifying the prompt. This could significantly impact reinforcement learning and robotics, especially in sparse-reward settings where policies rarely succeed. PDE resembles posterior sampling, a classical RL exploration framework, but operates at the level of prompts: the VLM maintains an implicit distribution over useful prompts and updates it from observed rollouts. The approach is a preprint without community discussion, and its effectiveness depends on the VLM's ability to reason over rollout videos.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning agents must explore to improve, but common methods add randomness to actions, which only yields small variations near the current policy. Vision-language-action (VLA) models condition robot policies on natural language prompts, so changing the prompt can cause large, global changes in behavior. The challenge is finding prompts that lead to useful behavior when rewards are too sparse to guide selection.

## [Data Imbalance Boosts Robust Generalization in Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10116) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.10116v2) reports that on a synthetic parity task with spurious correlations, a 2-layer, 2-head transformer reached 100% adversarial accuracy in 77% of seeds at a spurious ratio of r=0.90, versus 0% of seeds at r=0.50. The authors use gradient conflict dynamics, circuit evolution, and QK/OV circuit ablations to characterize a mechanistic pathway by which imbalance saturates shortcut features and promotes generalization. This counterintuitive finding challenges the common assumption that data imbalance is purely harmful, suggesting that under spurious correlations it can act as an implicit regularizer for sufficiently capable models. It has implications for robustness research, dataset design, and mechanistic interpretability of transformers. The effect is absent in 1-layer models, where imbalance instead traps the model on the shortcut, indicating that model capacity is critical. The analysis is limited to a synthetic task with a specific shortcut (parity of the maximum-valued element) and a specific architecture, so generalization to real-world tasks remains to be tested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Spurious correlations occur when a model learns a shortcut feature that is correlated with the true label in training but not causally related to it, causing failures under distribution shift. Mechanistic interpretability aims to reverse-engineer neural networks into human-understandable circuits, such as QK (query-key) circuits that determine attention patterns and OV (output-value) circuits that determine what information is moved. This paper combines both lenses to study how training data composition affects which circuits a transformer learns.
