## [Multi-Axis Max@K RL Boosts Diversity in Text-to-Image Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14962) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper (arXiv:2607.14962, accepted to WACV 2027) introduces multi-axis max@K, a group-based reinforcement learning objective that improves target-mode coverage in diffusion-based text-to-image models. Validated on SD3.5-M, it raises the Fairness Score by 0.23-0.36 over the base model across three automatic evaluators while preserving image quality and text alignment. Text-to-image models often collapse to a narrow subset of visual modes for a given prompt, which limits diversity and can amplify demographic skew in person-centric generations. This objective offers a practical way to improve representative diversity and fairness without sacrificing quality, relevant to anyone deploying generative image systems. Multi-axis max@K takes the maximum score across samples for each target mode and then sums these per-mode maxima, so a sample receives positive weight on a mode only when it raises that mode's group maximum, letting different samples contribute to different modes. The credit-assignment mechanism was validated on a synthetic mixture and on SD3.5-M with deterministic pixel-based color rewards, and code is available at https://github.com/KuOnoda/multi-axis-maxk.

**Background**: Diffusion-based text-to-image models such as Stable Diffusion 3.5 generate images by iteratively denoising random noise conditioned on a text prompt. Reinforcement learning with group-based objectives (like max@K) is increasingly used to fine-tune such models toward desired properties. Target-mode coverage refers to how well a model's samples span a predefined set of semantically specified modes, such as different perceived appearances.

## [LLMs Evaluated on 130 Obfuscated Security Protocols](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20712) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.20712v2) evaluates GPT and DeepSeek in both chat and reasoning modes across three runs on 130 obfuscated AnB/AnBx protocols covering 388 security goals, scoring them against the formal tools ProVerif and OFMC. Chat models achieved high recall but low precision (GPT: 72.7% recall, 27.3% precision), while enabling reasoning flipped the trade-off toward higher precision but lower recall (GPT reasoning: 66.5% precision, 54.5% recall). This study provides a rigorous quantitative benchmark at the intersection of AI and formal security verification, showing that LLMs still cannot match established tools like ProVerif and OFMC. The findings suggest LLMs may at best serve as pre-screening filters, which is important guidance for security researchers and practitioners considering LLM-assisted protocol analysis. The goal set is highly imbalanced (89 vulnerable vs. 299 secure goals), and a trivial always-secure predictor scores 77.1% accuracy, which only GPT reasoning exceeds. All models perform worst on authentication goals, verdicts are unstable across runs (identical on only 61.6%–89.7% of goals), and self-reported confidence shows no meaningful correlation with correctness; results rest on a single zero-shot prompt and two providers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Security protocol verification traditionally relies on formal tools such as ProVerif, which uses Horn clause resolution over a symbolic model of cryptography, and OFMC, a back-end of the AVISPA tool suite. Protocols are often specified in languages like AnB and its extension AnBx, which are based on the informal Alice & Bob notation and add richer communication channel abstractions. This paper asks whether large language models can perform comparable symbolic analysis without such dedicated formal machinery.

It also raises the striking possibility that LLM trainees can outperform humans in simulated therapeutic competence, which could reshape how therapists are trained and evaluated. Supervisor feedback improved diagnostic accuracy in 5 of 7 LLM conditions, and symptom identification accuracy increased with model size. The authors caution that patient fidelity, supervisor calibration, and harmful feedback still require evaluation through a complex systems perspective, and the paper is an arXiv preprint without peer review.

A new arXiv paper (2608.04765v3) proposes a hierarchical vision-language-action architecture with an explicit language-memory module that converts discrete temporal observations into a coherent textual memory sequence with temporal logic. The system decouples a high-level VLM for semantic reasoning from a low-level VLA for continuous control, and the high-level VLM recursively updates both language memory and subtask instructions using the previous memory as a contextual anchor. By making memory explicit and textual, this work offers an interpretable route to better temporal consistency and closed-loop error correction, potentially benefiting embodied AI and real-world robot deployment. The high-level VLM is trained via a visual question answering paradigm, while the low-level VLA executes precise continuous control conditioned on subtask instructions and visual observations. ## [Type Vectors Let LLMs Model Human Economic Choices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18265) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.18265v3) introduces a general AI-based modeling technique that assigns large language models a vector of trait intensities—a "type vector"—and asks them to make choices, then fits the trait values to match observed human decisions. Applied to 119,147 decisions from 78,657 subjects across more than 35 countries and 10 classic economic game roles, the method shows human behavior can be closely matched using just three dimensions: Risk Aversion, Strategic Sophistication, and Trust. This work bridges the flexibility and interpretability of natural language with a mathematically fittable structure, offering behavioral economists, cognitive scientists, and AI researchers a new tool for modeling human choice that is both quantitative and human-readable. Because the fitted type vectors can predict behavior in held-out games with different rules and actions, the approach could generalize to domains well beyond economic games, such as marketing, policy design, and user simulation. The type vectors needed to fit individuals across games cluster into fewer than a dozen groups, though fit quality varies substantially across subjects. The authors emphasize that the method is easy to implement and highly generalizable—any vector of traits can be input to model behavior in any setting—but the paper does not claim a paradigm shift, and the coarse 1–5 trait scale may limit fine-grained resolution.

"Type vectors" address this by encoding personality-like traits (e.g., Altruism: 2 out of 5, Risk Aversion: 4 out of 5) as numbers that can be systematically varied and fitted to data, similar in spirit to recent work on personality vectors that modulate LLM behavior without retraining. Classic economic games—such as dictator, trust, and public-goods games—provide standardized settings where human choices are already well documented, making them a natural testbed for such models.

## [PonderPounce Uses Pretrained MLLM as Episode Memory for Robot Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24115) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PonderPounce introduces a dual-system architecture in which PONDER, a pretrained System 2 multimodal LLM, integrates episode history and demonstrations to produce continuous cognition, while POUNCE, a System 1 action model, asynchronously conditions control on the newest cognition and its age. Both are jointly trained end to end without separate bridge pretraining, achieving 60.83% success on RoboMME at base data scale and 75.54% with 9x data, versus 44.51% and 57.88% for FrameSamp+Modul. This work shows that a pretrained MLLM's native causal context can serve as episode memory for robot control, avoiding purpose-built memory modules and offering a simpler path to long-horizon manipulation. The strong RoboMME results and real-world applicability suggest the approach could influence how vision-language-action systems handle temporal context. Optimized per-call inference on an H100 achieves p50 latencies of 78 ms for cognition-only refresh and 25 ms for action-model invocation; scaling PONDER from 0.8B to 9B adds 6.71 percentage points at base scale with POUNCE unchanged, while a separately trained 9B PONDER without execution history reaches only 26.21%. The system also achieves 12.5% success on RoboCasa-DC and 60.98% mean success on four real-world tasks under asynchronous execution, versus 40.67% for FrameSamp+Modul.

## [Curvature Cryptanalysis Recovers Transformer FFN Directions via Hessians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28843) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.28843v2) introduces a curvature-based cryptanalysis method that recovers hidden feed-forward network (FFN) directions in smooth transformers using projected input Hessians and black-box queries. On independently trained CIFAR-10 vision transformers, only 16 projected Hessians (8193 black-box queries) recover FFN directions with average absolute cosine alignment above 0.91, achieving a 16x reduction in query cost via vector-output stencil reuse. This work exposes a novel second-order leakage channel for model extraction of transformer FFNs, showing that smooth activation functions like GELU and SiLU leak internal parameter geometry that behavioral fidelity alone cannot reveal. It has significant implications for AI security and privacy, as it enables high-fidelity functional extraction with over 92% top-1 agreement while test accuracy remains within 1.24% and 0.57% of the GELU and SiLU targets. The method formalizes Hessian collection as a partially symmetric tensor decomposition to establish local identifiability and stability conditions, and exploits vector-output stencil reuse to cut structural query cost by 16x. Recovery remains high across independently trained models, repeated extraction runs, and all transformer blocks, though output rounding and Gaussian noise substantially reduce recovery under a fixed attack configuration—adapting the finite-difference step restores average alignment to 0.9188 and 0.9081.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer feed-forward networks (FFNs) are two-layer MLPs with smooth activations such as GELU or SiLU, which are differentiable and thus expose curvature information. The Hessian matrix describes the local curvature of a function via second-order partial derivatives, and projected Hessians form mixtures of hidden symmetric rank-one factors induced by FFN input weights. Partially symmetric tensor decomposition provides the mathematical framework for identifying these hidden factors from black-box observations.

## [TGOPD Gates On-Policy Distillation by Prompt-Level Teacher Reliability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02998) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.02998v2) introduces Teacher-Gated On-Policy Distillation (TGOPD), which estimates teacher reliability from a small set of verifier-scored teacher probes and routes each prompt either to dense on-policy distillation (OPD) when the reliability check passes or to verifier-grounded GRPO otherwise. Across 4B and 35B students in mathematics, code, and instruction following, TGOPD outperforms Vanilla OPD in all six single-domain settings and achieves higher seven-benchmark averages at both scales under multi-domain training. On-policy distillation is becoming a standard, cost-efficient post-training technique, but its uniform application of dense teacher supervision can propagate confidently wrong teacher signals into the student. TGOPD's prompt-level verification offers a practical safeguard that could improve reliability of distillation pipelines and, by using otherwise-idle teacher capacity, raise teacher-node GPU utilization from 9.8% to 78.9% in the measured 4B single-domain run. The method is motivated by the fact that reverse KL is mode-seeking, so a confidently wrong teacher can induce a strong yet misleading update, while distributional proxies such as entropy or teacher-student likelihood agreement measure uncertainty or agreement but do not directly verify outcome correctness. TGOPD therefore relies on verifier-scored teacher probes rather than distributional proxies, and its reported gains span 4B and 35B students across mathematics, code, and instruction following.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) accelerates post-training by having the student sample its own rollouts while a frozen teacher provides dense token-level log-probability supervision on those student-visited prefixes, which reduces the train-inference distribution gap of off-policy distillation. GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement-learning algorithm that drops PPO's value network and uses the average reward of a sampled group of answers per prompt as the baseline. Reverse KL divergence is called mode-seeking because minimizing it tends to concentrate the learned distribution on one high-density mode of the target rather than covering all modes.

A new arXiv paper introduces HAE-GEO, a benchmark that evaluates search-augmented LLM agents under progressively persuasive web poisoning across three attack levels (L1 direct assertion, L2 contextual camouflage, L3 apparent corroboration). It uses a controlled corpus of 72,039 clean pages and 770 poisoned pages per level spanning 8 product categories and 154 brands, and evaluates 10 agents using deterministic behavioral measures plus six semantic rubric dimensions. Existing benchmarks mostly measure whether manipulated content is retrieved or endorsed, but not whether an agent verifies suspicious evidence, revises adopted claims, or recovers before giving a final recommendation. HAE-GEO fills this gap by tracking the full trajectory from exposure to recovery, offering AI safety and agent robustness researchers a tool to study adversarial manipulation of search-augmented agents used for consumer decisions. The benchmark uses a multi-turn Search-Scrape interface, and evaluating 10 agents revealed three recurring patterns: evidence recognition degrades under the corroboration trap, agentic search improves final resistance without improving evidence recognition or utility, and defense prompting increases verification but rarely converts verification into recovery.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Generative Engine Optimization (GEO) is the practice of optimizing content so it gets cited by AI search engines and assistants such as ChatGPT, Perplexity, and Google AI Overviews; malicious GEO poisoning plants manipulated content to steer AI-generated answers. Search-augmented LLM agents retrieve and scrape web pages to inform recommendations, making them vulnerable to such poisoning. HAE-GEO is a benchmark designed to measure how well these agents resist, verify, and recover from poisoned evidence.

<a id="item-259"></a>
## [Sparse Data Augmentation Achieves Provable Optimization Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08133) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.08133v2) proves that gradient descent on a sparsely augmented objective, using a small fixed sample of transformations drawn before optimization, can return an ε-stationary point of the fully augmented objective with probability at least 1−δ. The method requires only O((log|G| + log(1/δ))/ε²) group-transformation-oracle queries, compared to O(1/ε⁴) for standard group stochastic gradient descent (group-SGD). This result significantly reduces the query complexity of data augmentation in geometric machine learning, where transformation groups can be enormous or only accessible via sampling. It offers a theoretically grounded alternative to full augmentation and group-SGD, potentially making invariance-promoting training far more efficient for large or continuous groups. The proof establishes a uniform approximation of the full group-averaged gradient field by a random group average, using spectral properties of group-induced operators and tools from representation theory. The guarantees hold under suitable regularity conditions and with high probability, but the work is a theoretical contribution without an immediate practical implementation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Geometric machine learning studies models that respect symmetries such as rotations, reflections, or permutations, often by averaging the loss over transformations of the data. Computing this fully augmented objective requires evaluating every element of the transformation group G, which is expensive when G is large or only sampleable. Group-SGD avoids this by sampling a fresh transformation each iteration, but its query cost grows quickly as the target accuracy improves. This paper asks whether a single small, fixed sample of transformations, reused throughout optimization, can suffice.

## [No-Box Vulnerability Analysis Detects Prompt Injection in MCP Servers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10854) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.10854v2) proposes 'no-box vulnerability analysis,' a paradigm that detects vulnerabilities using only functionality metadata, with no system access or runtime interaction. The authors implement a prototype called MCPSEC that audits Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers for indirect prompt injection using only tool metadata exposed at server registration time, identifying 143 vulnerable tools across 20 servers and 177 tools, with 94 (98.9% recall) matching human-verified vulnerabilities versus 80 (84.2% recall) for an LLM baseline. This work addresses a real gap for auditing closed-source, remotely hosted, or commercially gated systems where third-party analysts have neither access nor interaction capability. As MCP servers proliferate as the connective layer between LLMs and external tools, metadata-only vulnerability detection could enable proactive security auditing before deployment. MCPSEC hypothesizes vulnerabilities that exist across all possible implementations consistent with a given system's metadata, producing testable attack scenarios for later validation when additional access becomes available. The paper's abstract is truncated, and no community discussion or independent replication is yet available to gauge the robustness of the approach.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is an open standard that lets large language models securely connect to external tools and data sources through MCP servers. Indirect prompt injection is an attack where malicious instructions are embedded in content the model processes, causing it to follow unintended commands. Traditional vulnerability analysis typically requires either source code access (white-box) or runtime interaction (black-box); 'no-box' analysis sits at the extreme end where only functionality metadata—inputs, outputs, and side effects—is available.

<a id="item-261"></a>
## [Modular aLoRA Framework Targets Specific LLM Harms During Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13624) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.13624v2) proposes a modular correction framework that augments pretrained LLMs with Activated LoRA (aLoRA) adapters and a context-aware router to eliminate specific harms such as bias or toxicity from model responses. The approach activates expert adapters mid-sequence without invalidating the KV cache, enabling low-latency, targeted correction during generation while preserving task performance on standard safety benchmarks. Existing alignment methods are costly and tightly coupled to the model, limiting flexibility and scalability; this modular design lets developers plug in specialized harm-mitigation experts without retraining the base model. If it holds up, it could offer a practical, lightweight path toward safer and more controllable LLM deployments, influencing future alignment and AI safety research. The framework trains each expert adapter to detect and mitigate a specific harm, while a learned router dynamically selects appropriate experts based on the model's intermediate outputs. The key technical trick is that aLoRA adapters only modify weights for tokens after they are invoked, so the existing KV cache remains valid and generation does not need to be restarted.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a lightweight fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable matrices to a frozen LLM instead of retraining all parameters. Activated LoRA (aLoRA), introduced by IBM Research in 2025, modifies LoRA so that adaptation only applies to tokens generated after the adapter is invoked, allowing reuse of the base model's KV cache for faster inference. The KV cache stores key and value vectors from previous tokens so transformers don't recompute them, but any change to an earlier token normally invalidates all subsequent cached vectors. This paper combines aLoRA with a routing mechanism to apply targeted safety corrections mid-generation without that cache penalty.

## [IWC-Bench: Testing-Based Benchmark for LLM-Generated Web Apps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15387) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released IWC-Bench, an interactive benchmark that evaluates LLM-generated web applications from a software testing perspective by instrumenting each app, using code coverage to guide user-simulated exploration, and abstracting interaction traces into a state-transition graph. It comprises 369 real-world user requirements and 5,088 acceptance criteria, and evaluation of 17 frontier LLMs shows no single model leads across visual aesthetics, usability, and requirement alignment, while achieving 85.3% agreement with human preferences on 197 validated arena sessions. This matters because existing static benchmarks can credit code that is unreachable at runtime, while interactive benchmarks may miss implemented functionality due to incomplete exploration or confuse app defects with agent failures. By separating exploration from scoring and grounding evaluation in runtime evidence, IWC-Bench offers a more reliable way to compare LLM web-generation capabilities, which is important for both AI/ML and software engineering communities. IWC-Bench instruments generated applications and uses code coverage to guide an agent's user-simulated interactions, then abstracts the trace into a state-transition graph for scoring along visual aesthetics, usability, and requirement alignment. Experiments show coverage guidance improves exploration coverage, and model rankings remain stable when the judge model is replaced, though the benchmark is a specialized academic contribution rather than an immediate industry-changing tool.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-generated web applications are increasingly common, but automatically judging their quality remains hard. Static benchmarks inspect source code and may reward functionality that never actually runs, while interactive benchmarks exercise the app but can explore incompletely or blame the testing agent for app bugs. IWC-Bench borrows ideas from software testing, such as code coverage-guided fuzzing and state-transition testing, to gather runtime evidence before scoring.

## [GAPA Dataset Shows 'Neutral' Physical Descriptions Still Carry Gender Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16366) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced GAPA (Gender Associations of Physical Attributes), a dataset of 316 common physical attributes paired with 14,706 gender-association ratings from 304 US-based annotators. Evaluating 16 LLMs against these human ratings, they found that models only partially recover human gender associations and show systematic misalignment, including compressed rating distributions and asymmetric abstention that disproportionately targets non-binary identities. This provides the first empirical evidence that replacing explicit gender labels with seemingly objective physical descriptions does not guarantee gender-neutral communication, challenging a common recommendation in AI fairness and accessibility work. The findings have direct implications for how foundation models describe people in human-AI interaction, and for fairness evaluation and debiasing efforts. The dataset contains 316 attributes and 14,706 ratings from 304 annotators, with more consistent and distinctive gender associations for women and men than for non-binary identities. The authors also release a best-performing proxy model that predicts human gender associations and demonstrate its utility in a sociolinguistic analysis of character descriptions in LitBank.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Recent work in AI fairness, accessibility, and ethics recommends that foundation models avoid inferred identity labels such as 'she' or 'his' and instead use seemingly objective physical descriptions like 'short hair' or 'a defined jawline.' Whether such descriptive language actually achieves gender-neutral communication had remained an open empirical question. GAPA is designed to test this assumption by measuring how human readers and LLMs associate gender with physical attributes.

## [MoRE: Sharing Expert Pools Across Adjacent MoE Layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18176v2) proposes Mixture of Reused Experts (MoRE), which shares expert pools across groups of adjacent Transformer layers while keeping a separate router in each layer. Lightweight learnable depth embeddings are added to condition each layer's input before routing, so shared experts can still distinguish which layer they are serving. Across three model scales (114M to 1.15B parameters), MoRE achieves lower perplexity and stronger downstream results than standard MoEs and state-of-the-art weight-sharing architectures at matched compute and parameter budgets. MoE models decouple capacity from compute but their memory footprint grows linearly with the number of experts, which is a major obstacle to scaling. MoRE shows that reusing expert pools across layers can improve quality without adding parameters, and it requires only minimal changes to existing MoE implementations, making it easy to adopt in large-scale LLM training pipelines. The design keeps a per-layer router but lets it select from a larger shared expert pool, which expands the number of possible routing combinations without extra parameters; depth embeddings are the mechanism that lets shared experts behave differently per layer. The paper reports consistent gains at three scales (114M, and up to 1.15B parameters) under matched compute and parameter budgets, though results are limited to these relatively small model sizes and to language modeling benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures split a model into many expert subnetworks and use a learned router to send each token to only a few of them, so total parameters can grow while per-token compute stays roughly fixed. Recurrent Transformers take the opposite approach to parameter efficiency: they reuse the same layer weights repeatedly across depth, but usually underperform standard Transformers in language modeling. Perplexity, the exponentiated cross-entropy of a model on held-out text, is the standard metric for comparing language models. MoRE combines these two ideas by sharing expert pools across adjacent layers rather than sharing whole layers.

## [M2Tok: Multi-head Multi-codebook Action Tokenizer for VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18259) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose M2Tok, a Multi-head Multi-codebook Action Tokenizer that decomposes latent action features into multiple heads and assigns each head an independent codebook for quantization, substantially reducing reconstruction loss compared to prior discrete action tokenizers. Evaluated on RoboTwin, Simpler-Env, and three zero-shot real-world tasks, the M2Tok-based VLA achieves higher reconstruction fidelity and significantly improved policy success rates, with code released at github.com/cpaaax/M2Tok. Discrete action tokenization is a recognized bottleneck that caps the performance ceiling of Vision-Language-Action models, which are increasingly used to let robots follow natural-language instructions. By expanding representational expressivity through combinatorial multi-codebook quantization, M2Tok could raise the success rate of downstream robotic policies and benefit researchers building general-purpose robot foundation models. The method introduces two structural innovations: decomposing latent action features into multiple heads that implicitly align with distinct action dimensions, and assigning independent codebooks per head so that the combinatorial nature of multiple codebooks expands representational expressivity. Ablation studies confirm the effectiveness of both the multi-head and multi-codebook mechanisms, and the approach is validated on RoboTwin, Simpler-Env, and three zero-shot real-world tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that take an image of a robot's surroundings plus a text instruction and directly output low-level robot actions, a concept pioneered by Google DeepMind's RT-2 in 2023. Because raw action signals are continuous, VLA systems typically tokenize them into discrete tokens so that autoregressive language-model architectures can process actions alongside images and text. Existing discrete action tokenizers, however, often suffer from high reconstruction loss, a "discretization bottleneck" that fails to preserve the fine-grained dynamics needed for precise control. Multi-codebook vector quantization generalizes classical vector quantization by representing a signal as a combination of codewords drawn from several codebooks, improving rate-distortion efficiency and robustness to codebook collapse.

Researchers present a pipeline that jointly leverages generated video and audio to derive motion trajectories and desired-force profiles for robot manipulation, shaping the force profile by the loudness of generated contact sound. They execute the resulting force-aware trajectories on a Franka robot using a closed-loop force regulator, achieving successful zero-shot manipulation on contact-rich tasks where a kinematic-only baseline fails, and also demonstrate the pipeline as a data generation engine for training closed-loop policies. This addresses a key limitation of video-based action extraction, which is purely kinematic and lacks force information essential for contact-rich tasks, potentially expanding zero-shot robot learning to tasks like wiping, pressing, and insertion. It also suggests a scalable path to generating force-aware training data without expensive real-world teleoperation. The force profile is derived from the loudness of generated contact audio and executed via a closed-loop force regulator on a Franka robot, with evaluation on multiple contact-requiring tasks. Project website, videos, and dataset are available at https://dreamingcontactsound.github.io/.

Zero-shot manipulation refers to performing tasks without task-specific training, while force-aware manipulation uses contact force feedback to succeed at tasks requiring physical interaction. The paper introduces BioPhys-Bridge, a benchmark dataset for evidence-grounded scientific reasoning over biophysical literature, with an initial release of 500 cases and 1,517 agent-facing tasks spanning six biological domains and nine physical model families. Preliminary evaluations show DeepSeek-V4-Flash achieving the highest evidence-ID F1 score of 0.360, followed by Qwen3.7-Max at 0.316 and GPT-4o-mini at 0.294. Each case includes evidence blocks, stable evidence IDs, quantitative values, units, equations, assumptions, mechanisms, and next decisions as grounding targets for QA and RAG. The dataset enforces strict quality gates for schema, evidence integrity, quantitative grounding, source license, duplicate detection, and unit normalization, with domain expert review for 81 cases.

Benchmarks like BioPhys-Bridge evaluate how well AI systems handle such multi-step reasoning across domains.

## [Paper Proposes Goals as Compositional Representations with Syntax and Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19448) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.19448v1) argues that goals in cognitive science and computer science should be viewed as compositional representations with a syntax and semantics, drawing a parallel to the syntax-semantics interface in linguistics and logic. The paper synthesizes existing work to characterize properties of different goal representations and frames them as points in a broader design space. This conceptual framework could help researchers in AI, cognitive science, and logic ask foundational questions about the expressivity, design, and efficiency of goal representations, potentially informing how artificial agents and cognitive models specify purposeful behavior. It also offers a way to make implicit assumptions about goals explicit by distinguishing their form from their meaning. The paper is a theoretical position piece without empirical results, so its impact is conceptual rather than immediately practical. It focuses on questions such as whether a particular goal language is expressive enough to capture behaviors of interest and whether different goal representations can capture the same behavior.

## [LLM-as-an-Improver: Verification Feedback Generates Better Candidates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19515) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19515) introduces LLM-as-an-Improver and proposes Verify-Repair-Reselect (VRR), a test-time method that uses verification feedback not only to rank candidates but also to generate improved ones. VRR retains the initial winner and conditionally produces three complementary alternatives: repaired versions of the winner and runner-up, plus a solution based on a new approach, then filters invalid/duplicate candidates and reselects the final answer. This work expands the role of verifiers from passive rankers to active generators of better solutions, potentially improving LLM performance on code generation and reasoning tasks without additional model training. It could benefit developers and researchers who rely on verifier-based selection pipelines, and it shows that correct solutions can be recovered even when all initial candidates are wrong. VRR uses only inference-time information to filter invalid and duplicate candidates, and it reselects the final answer under the original evaluation criteria. The paper reports improvements over fixed-pool verifier-based selection across diverse models and benchmarks, though the gains are described as occurring 'in many settings' rather than universally.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Verifier-based selection is a common test-time technique where an LLM generates multiple candidate solutions and a verifier picks the most promising one. Traditional methods treat verification as a one-time ranking step and discard its feedback after evaluating a fixed candidate pool. VRR builds on this by reusing verification diagnostics to construct additional candidates, a paradigm the authors call LLM-as-an-Improver.

Low-altitude wireless networks are becoming critical infrastructure for concurrent drone services, but conventional controllers rely on predefined objectives and cannot adapt to time-varying service requirements. The architecture treats the coexistence of mobility, connectivity, and shared resources as a dynamic non-cooperative game whose operating conditions and coordination objectives evolve over time. ## [Benchmarking LLM Travel Agents for Itinerary Revision Under Disruptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19654) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19654) presents a systematic empirical evaluation of three approaches to revising travel itineraries after disruptions: LLM-Z3 full replanning, IPyHOPPER hierarchical plan repair, and an iTIMO local-revision adapter. Using two TREK-derived benchmark sets — 500 single-disruption cases (feasible and infeasible) and 200 feasible simultaneous compound-disruption cases — the study compares effectiveness, plan stability, and computational cost. Travel-planning agents built on LLMs can produce itineraries that become infeasible after acceptance due to flight cancellations, hotel unavailability, or attraction closures, yet the differing task formulations and evaluation protocols of replanning, plan repair, and LLM revision have hindered fair comparison. This study offers practical guidelines for balancing feasibility recovery, commitment preservation, and computational cost, which is valuable for researchers and engineers building robust LLM-based planning agents. LLM-Z3 with Gemini achieved the highest observed compound-disruption success, while IPyHOPPER nearly matched that configuration's single-disruption overall success and preserved substantially more of the accepted itinerary on successful repairs. Computational profiles differed sharply: IPyHOPPER used no LLM inference, the evaluated LLM-Z3 adapter used compact one-call inference, and the iTIMO adapter consumed substantially more tokens.

## [MetaRTL: Meta-path Attention for Scalable Relational Table Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19832) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MetaRTL introduces a two-stage framework for relational table learning that first obtains initial table embeddings through lightweight pre-training, then performs non-parametric message passing to derive meta-path features aggregated by a new attention module called MetaAttn. Experiments on 10 real-world datasets across 24 tasks show it outperforms deep GNN and HGNN stacks while maintaining high efficiency. Relational databases hold the majority of enterprise data, yet existing graph-based methods are computationally expensive and scale poorly to large real-world databases. By shifting computation from deep message passing to efficient meta-path aggregation, MetaRTL could make relational table learning more practical for large-scale industrial applications. The framework avoids deep GNN/HGNN stacks by using non-parametric message passing to derive meta-path features, which reduces computational cost while capturing rich relational semantics. The approach was validated on 10 real-world datasets across 24 tasks, though it remains an arXiv preprint without peer review or released code at this time.

Existing approaches often model tables as heterogeneous information networks and use graph neural networks (GNNs) with message passing, where nodes iteratively aggregate information from neighbors. A meta-path is a composite relation connecting different node types in such a network, capturing higher-order semantics that plain edges miss.

A new arXiv paper proposes a Physics Informed Recurrent Neural Network (PIRNN) that predicts unobservable intermediate physical variables alongside the target, both on historical data and forecast horizons. The method is tested on groundwater level prediction using the Gardenia physical model across twelve real-world datasets, outperforming other models on five of them. This work challenges the common PINN approach of enforcing physical constraints on forecasts, arguing that embedding physical knowledge into historical data is more effective. It could improve interpretability and robustness in scientific machine learning applications like hydrology, where domain experts need to understand intermediate physical processes. The PIRNN is adaptable to any physical model described by multiple equations, each with its own set of unobservable variables. An ablation study confirms the importance of physical background, and a domain expert assessed the coherence of the predicted physical variables.

## [TRACE: Training-Free Agentic Retrieval for Historical Archive Source Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19897) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced TRACE, a training-free agentic retrieval framework for accountable source discovery over OCR-degraded historical corpora, developed within the DECIDON project on French Third Republic political discourse. Deployed internally to 24 researchers across six partner institutions, it achieves R@10 = 0.856 and MRR = 0.653 on the new HistoriQA-ThirdRepublic benchmark of 1,752 French historical questions, outperforming sparse, dense, graph-based, and agentic RAG baselines at roughly $0.02 per question. It shows that accountable, corpus-aware agent design can match or beat heavier training-based and graph-construction retrieval approaches for large digital libraries, without requiring costly local GPU infrastructure. This matters for heritage institutions, laboratories, and small companies that need traceable sources for scholarly and institutional use but lack large compute budgets. The benchmark draws on 1,752 French questions over parliamentary debates and newspapers from 1887, with documents derived from Bibliothèque nationale de France digitised collections, and the largest gains appear on multi-hop and cross-corpus questions. The system is training-free and runs under a default hosted inference configuration at about $0.02 per question, though it is currently only an internally deployed prototype rather than a public release.

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is a technique in which a large language model first retrieves relevant documents from an external corpus and then generates an answer grounded in them, which reduces hallucination and allows source citation. Agentic retrieval extends this by using an LLM-driven multi-query pipeline that decomposes complex questions and iteratively searches, rather than issuing a single retrieval pass. Historical archives are a hard case for such systems because OCR errors, heterogeneous genres, and the need for scholarly traceability complicate both retrieval and attribution.

## [MaSCoD: Multi-Agent Framework Improves LLM Causal Graph Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19944) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose MaSCoD, a multi-agent framework that organizes candidate third variables and local structural patterns before making direct-edge judgments, explicitly targeting the premature omission of causal relations in LLM-based causal discovery. Evaluated on Auto-MPG, DWD, and Sachs with GPT-5.4 and GPT-4o, the Full configuration achieved higher mean Recall and F1 than the No Phase 1 variant across all six dataset-backbone settings, though with increased false-positive rates. This work reframes premature omission as an explicit design and evaluation objective for LLM-based causal discovery, which could shift how researchers build and benchmark candidate-graph generation pipelines. The nuanced, dataset- and backbone-dependent results suggest that structural pre-organization is a promising but not universally superior strategy, informing future multi-agent causal discovery systems. The Full configuration supplies structural hypotheses before direct-edge judgment, while No Phase 1 constructs them within the judgment procedure; additional reference-edge retention over baselines was observed only on DWD with GPT-5.4 and Sachs with GPT-4o, not uniformly. Partial ablations showed that supplying both information components did not always outperform supplying only one, and stage-wise analysis for GPT-5.4 found the retention gap already present after direct-edge judgment, with reconciliation introducing extra reference-edge loss on Sachs.

## [Deep Architecture Jointly Optimizes Cost Functions and Route Ranking for Customizable Route Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19996) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19996) proposes a deep architecture that jointly optimizes cost functions and a route-ranking model for customizable route planning. It uses an offline multi-objective Dijkstra algorithm to collect Pareto-optimal routes as a complete candidate set, then trains an end-to-end differentiable network that emulates shortest-path search and route ranking, with a novel constrained-optimization loss for route preferences. This work tackles the feedback-loop problem in data-driven route planning, where user trajectories are biased by the navigation service itself, and could improve route quality and personalization for online navigation and ride-hailing platforms. It also advances differentiable shortest-path search, a growing direction at the intersection of graph algorithms and deep learning. The architecture integrates a differentiable shortest-path search module with a downstream route-ranking module, and defines route preference as constrained optimization of route attributes, optimizing a single objective while keeping other variables strictly under constraints. Experiments on real-world datasets reportedly show significant improvements over state-of-the-art methods in route quality and customizability, though the abstract does not specify dataset scale or computational cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Classic shortest-path algorithms such as Dijkstra use heuristic cost functions to define edge weights, which gives no guarantee that the resulting route matches user preferences. Prior data-driven approaches often treat user trajectories as ground truth for the optimal route, but because those trajectories are influenced by the navigation service, this creates a feedback loop that biases learning. Differentiable shortest-path search aims to make pathfinding trainable end-to-end so that costs can be learned from data, and Pareto-optimal routes are those for which no other route is better on all objectives simultaneously.

## [FedeRICo: Federated Region-Influenced Coupling for Traffic Flow Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20026) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (arXiv:2609.20026) introduces FedeRICo, a federated traffic forecasting framework that combines gradient-level collaboration with boundary-aware residual communication. It uses a dual-branch architecture—a globally guided branch coordinated via gradient alignment and a private residual branch that exchanges only transient spatial-temporal residual signals between physically adjacent clients—to handle heterogeneous client sensor subgraphs and recover cross-boundary spatial dependencies. Federated traffic forecasting is important because urban traffic data is often siloed across stakeholders who cannot share raw data due to privacy or commercial constraints. FedeRICo addresses two long-standing limitations of existing federated spatial-temporal methods—parameter aggregation diluting client-specific representations and road network partitioning breaking cross-boundary traffic dynamics—and reportedly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on four real-world benchmarks. The framework extracts boundary messages through a trend-residual decomposition that suppresses periodic structure and communicates only transient spatial-temporal residual signals between physically adjacent clients. Experiments across four real-world traffic forecasting benchmarks show consistent improvements over state-of-the-art federated spatial-temporal baselines while maintaining competitive training runtime.

**Background**: Federated learning is a machine learning technique in which multiple entities (clients) collaboratively train a model while keeping their data decentralized, rather than centrally stored; a defining characteristic is data heterogeneity, since client data may not be independently and identically distributed. Graph neural networks (GNNs) are neural networks designed for graph-structured inputs, where nodes iteratively update their representations by exchanging information with neighbors via message passing. Spatial-temporal forecasting predicts future values in systems that vary over both space and time, such as traffic sensor networks, and federated spatial-temporal approaches apply federated learning to such forecasting tasks.

## [MAGMA-GEN Turns Ambiguous Robot Failures into Validated Recovery Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20056) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MAGMA-GEN is an on-policy data-generation pipeline that converts ambiguous failed rollouts in hierarchical robotic systems into validated recovery supervision. It uses a privileged coach to hypothesize early decision-level errors and propose localized corrections, then retains only candidates that improve downstream progress when re-executed from the same state under matched conditions. This addresses a real bottleneck in long-horizon robot learning: failed rollouts are ambiguous, and traditional supervised learning lacks recovery data while reinforcement learning suffers from sparse rewards and non-local credit assignment. By generating supervision from the agent's own failure distribution without per-step human demonstrations, MAGMA-GEN could improve task success and recovery in both simulation and real-robot execution. The privileged coach's diagnosis is fallible, so candidate corrections are validated by counterfactual re-execution from the same state under matched conditions, ensuring only genuinely helpful recoveries become supervised examples. The method is evaluated on interactive long-horizon manipulation tasks against distillation and trajectory-repair baselines under evolving task constraints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Hierarchical robotic systems decompose long-horizon manipulation into high-level semantic decisions that orchestrate stochastic low-level skills. When such a system fails, it is often unclear whether the high-level decision was invalid, the observation was partial, or a valid decision simply failed in physical execution. MAGMA-GEN targets this ambiguity by generating recovery supervision from the agent's own failures rather than relying on human demonstrations or sparse-reward reinforcement learning.

## [FCA-Guided Counterfactual Explanations Achieve Perfect Validity for Breast Cancer Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20067) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces the FCA-Guided Counterfactual (FCA-CF) framework, which uses a Formal Concept Analysis concept lattice as a hard structural constraint on counterfactual search over the multi-modal TCGA-BRCA dataset. It achieves Validity = 1.0000, Sparsity = 2.37 features changed, and Proximity = 0.900, outperforming four genuine counterfactual methods (Wachter-style CF, DiCE, FACE, and NICE) on 60 benign-predicted instances. Counterfactual explanations that are valid and sparse are essential for making deep learning-based breast cancer diagnosis clinically acceptable, since clinicians need actionable alternative scenarios rather than mere feature attributions. This work shows that structural constraints from lattice topology can produce Pareto-dominant explanations, potentially advancing explainable AI in high-stakes healthcare settings. The classifier achieves Accuracy = 0.980, F1 = 0.976, and ROC-AUC = 0.9947, and ablation analysis confirms the FCA lattice constraint is the primary sparsity driver (removing it increases sparsity by +40%, p < 0.001, Cohen's d = 0.78), while Phase C greedy refinement contributes the largest individual effect (+113% sparsity increase when disabled, p < 0.001, d = 5.01). The emergent sparsity arises from lattice topology rather than numerical penalty terms, which the authors highlight as a structurally novel contribution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Formal Concept Analysis (FCA) is a mathematical method that organizes data into a concept lattice, a hierarchy of formal concepts derived from objects and their attributes. Counterfactual explanations in machine learning describe how minimally changing input features would flip a model's prediction, offering actionable insights. The TCGA-BRCA dataset is a collection of breast invasive carcinoma data from The Cancer Genome Atlas, linking clinical images and genomics for cancer research.

## [Paper Unifies Marginal Utility, Matrix Factorization, and KV Cache](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20068) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20068) proposes a unified information-economic framework showing that the singular value spectrum of a rating matrix, the eigenvalue spectrum of a projected covariance operator, and transformer KV cache eviction/compression all reduce to a single allocation rule: retain the top dimensions whose eigenvalue exceeds the shadow price of the binding memory constraint. The paper applies this framework to geo-mining document extraction, reporting an 11.2-million-parameter hierarchical classifier that reaches 90.0% level-1 accuracy on a 973-document uranium-exploration corpus, trained in about five minutes on a single GPU at 2.62 ms per card, and diagnoses a degenerate mode in uniform-density TIES model merging. If the proposed equivalence holds, it would give researchers a single economic lens for reasoning about model compression, latent factor selection, and inference-time memory management, potentially guiding more principled KV cache eviction and low-rank compression strategies. The geo-mining application also demonstrates that small, cheaply trained models can approach proprietary API accuracy at a fraction of the latency and cost, which matters for domain-specific extraction pipelines. The framework's core rule is to retain dimensions whose eigenvalue exceeds the shadow price of the binding constraint, and the paper motivates a multi-pass inference protocol, a layer-wise TIES merging procedure, and a selection policy scalarized with a Conditional Value-at-Risk term on localization drift. Notably, the full-scale extraction benchmark including LoRA fine-tuning is reported as projected rather than measured, and the 92.0% proprietary-model comparison comes from a fifty-document human audit rather than the full corpus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: The KV cache stores key and value vectors for every layer, head, and token during transformer inference, and its memory cost often dominates inference economics at long context lengths. Matrix factorization techniques such as singular value decomposition (SVD) decompose matrices into spectra of singular values, which are commonly used for dimensionality reduction and latent factor analysis. TIES-Merging is a three-step model-merging method that trims redundant parameters, resolves sign conflicts, and averages parameters to combine task-specific models without retraining. Marginal utility is an economics concept describing the additional satisfaction gained from one more unit of a resource, and shadow price refers to the implicit cost of a binding constraint.

## [SAT-Based Framework Solves Minimum Span Antibandwidth Labeling Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20091) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces the Minimum Span Antibandwidth/Cyclic Antibandwidth Labeling (MSABL/MSCABL) problems, a complementary perspective to the classic Antibandwidth and Cyclic Antibandwidth problems, and develops a unified Boolean Satisfiability (SAT)-based framework to solve them. It formulates MSABL/MSCABL as a sequence of decision problems and exploits monotonicity to accelerate search, evaluating parallel and incremental SAT solving strategies on Harwell-Boeing Sparse Matrix Collection benchmarks against CPLEXCP, CPLEXMIP, and Gurobi. This work shows that SAT solving can serve as a highly competitive exact approach for these NP-hard graph labeling problems, potentially expanding the toolkit available to researchers in combinatorial optimization and SAT solving. It also highlights the value of the minimum span perspective, which had received comparatively little attention compared to the classic maximization formulation. The framework exploits monotonicity to accelerate search, with the parallel SAT strategy examining multiple candidate spans concurrently and the incremental strategy reusing a single SAT instance while progressively restricting the label domain. Results show the parallel approach performs best overall for MSCABL and the incremental approach for MSABL, and with the no-hole constraint the SAT-based methods remain competitive with CPLEXCP while significantly outperforming CPLEXMIP and Gurobi, particularly for MSCABL.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: The Antibandwidth problem is an NP-hard graph labeling problem that places the vertices of a graph on consecutive integer points so as to maximize the minimum difference between labels assigned to adjacent vertices, with applications in scheduling, radio frequency assignment, obnoxious facility location, and map-coloring. The Boolean Satisfiability (SAT) problem asks whether there exists an assignment of truth values that satisfies a given Boolean formula, and modern SAT solvers are widely used as exact engines for hard combinatorial problems. This paper flips the classic antibandwidth objective: instead of maximizing the minimum distance, it fixes a prescribed minimum (cyclic) distance and minimizes the label span.

This paper proposes an AI-driven Learning-to-Optimise (L2O) framework based on Graph Neural Networks for real-time multi-hop relay selection in NR-V2X networks. It uses an offline Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP) formulation to generate optimal relay decisions as supervision for training an edge-aware Graph Isomorphism Network with Edge Features (GINE), achieving up to 11.3% connectivity gain and up to 100x speed-up over MILP. Reliable low-latency communication is critical for smart city services and Industry 4.0 applications, but limited Road-Side Unit deployment and complex urban propagation often disrupt connectivity for Connected and Automated Vehicles. This work shows that learned graph-based optimization can deliver near-optimal relay decisions in real time, enabling scalable network control for urban deployments. The vehicular network is modelled as a graph where nodes represent CAVs and RSUs and edges encode radio-link characteristics, and the GINE architecture is specifically chosen to exploit edge features. Experiments on realistic urban datasets show near-optimal connectivity performance, though the approach relies on offline MILP supervision and may face generalization challenges across different urban scenarios.

## [Task Information Boosts AI Planning Success Under Token Limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20449) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20449) reports controlled workflow experiments on externally verified software-engineering tasks, showing that direct execution achieves the same 59.6% success rate at both 12,000 and 24,000 logical-token ceilings, while information-constrained planning improves from 36.2% to 51.2% as the budget doubles. A strict read-only planning campaign further found that giving the planner access to the task issue raised success by roughly 16 percentage points at 12,000 tokens. The findings suggest that how task information and token capacity are distributed across planning and execution stages—not just raw model scale—determines the economic value of AI inference, which matters for teams designing agentic coding workflows and allocating compute budgets. It implies that simply increasing token ceilings may not help direct execution, but can substantially benefit planning-based pipelines. The planning disadvantage narrowed by 15.0 percentage points (95% task-cluster bootstrap interval: 4.2 to 25.8), and at 24,000 tokens task-informed planning showed a 29.6-point advantage over direct execution, while at 12,000 tokens it was about 10 points lower. Direct execution used substantially less than either ceiling, whereas the planning workflow's binding rate fell from 46.2% to 0.8% and downstream execution accounted for 89.9% of the increase in total token use.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Logical-token ceilings refer to the maximum number of tokens an AI system may consume for a task, a common constraint in LLM-based workflows. In agentic software engineering, a planner model decomposes a task into steps that an executor model then carries out, and the paper studies how giving the planner access to the original task issue affects outcomes. The task-cluster bootstrap is a statistical resampling method that accounts for the nested structure of tasks grouped into clusters when computing confidence intervals.

## [Claim-Safe Protocol for Closed-Loop AI Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20538) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a claim-safe protocol with three actions—Refuse, Decompose, Refresh—for closed-loop AI evaluation, instantiated in an aggregate-only simulator with 24 policy components, three demand regimes, two fault-mask families, and a preregistered heldout of 1,440 cases and 21,600 partition rows. Only 55 of 72 regime-component units were reference-admitted, and 54 of 55 remained runtime-admitted, making abstention part of the result. The work addresses a subtle but important gap in AI evaluation: perfect reproducibility does not guarantee that an evaluation supports a valid claim, especially in closed-loop systems where the policy determines visited states and observable failures. If adopted, the protocol could change how practitioners report evaluation outcomes, moving away from single PASS/FAIL labels toward decomposed, reference-relative claims. Stable false admission was 0/20 represented components with a one-sided exact 95% upper bound of 0.1391 under a frozen 0.20 rule, and within admitted units affected clean traffic outpredicted nominal fault-cell fraction by 0.1264 nats per row (95% component-cluster interval [0.0593, 0.1918]). A drift log showed that clean fault-null streams triggered 15/15, 0/15, and 14/15 alarms across three regimes, so only the middle regime matched the frozen detector reference, underscoring that "null" must be reference-relative.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Closed-loop AI evaluation refers to settings where a policy's actions influence which states are visited and which failures become observable, making evaluation outcomes dependent on the system's own behavior. Distribution shift occurs when the data or environment encountered at deployment differs from the reference conditions used during development, which can invalidate previously calibrated detectors. This paper argues that reproducibility alone is insufficient for valid claims and proposes an executable contract linking observable support, statistical calibration, and justified claims.

## [Cell Role Annotation Framework Hits Ceiling for Spreadsheet RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20732) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.20732) proposes a framework that splits any spreadsheet into interpretable chunks using cell role annotation, beating the state of the art for LLM-driven RAG. However, the authors show the approach faces a hard ceiling because spreadsheets are fundamentally two-dimensional with continuous relationships and infinite potential cell roles, which finite classification models cannot fully capture. This work identifies a fundamental bottleneck in feeding spreadsheets to LLMs and argues the field must move beyond discrete cell classification toward dimensionality-reduction techniques that flatten 2D spreadsheets into 1D text. It could reshape how RAG pipelines handle tabular data and steer future research on spreadsheet understanding. The framework improves chunking interpretability and answer generation through enriched context rather than improving retrieval accuracy, and the authors note that even human-level annotation cannot overcome the limitation of finite, pre-defined cell classes. They therefore call for dimensionality-reduction methods that directly flatten 2D unstructured spreadsheets into 1D unstructured text that downstream RAG can more easily interpret.

Cell role annotation assigns semantic labels (such as header, data, or label) to cells to make chunks more interpretable, while dimensionality reduction refers to transforming high-dimensional data into a lower-dimensional representation that preserves meaningful properties.

Researchers introduced RAFT (Retrieval-Augmented Framework for Troubleshooting Agents), a stateful RAG framework that abstracts each closed historical support case into a directed chain of timeline entries and retrieves at the entry level rather than treating cases as static documents. RAFT improves Case Hit over vanilla RAG and GraphRAG baselines at every stage of case progress, with statistically significant gains over the strongest baseline, and the authors release their benchmark, implementation, and Apache Jira evaluation set. Enterprise support agents often need guidance that matches the current intermediate state of an ongoing problem, not just the final resolution of a similar past case, so entry-level retrieval could make troubleshooting assistants substantially more useful. The work also targets a real gap in RAG research, where multi-stage, stateful processes are usually flattened into single documents, and it offers a retrieval-layer evaluation that avoids the cost of production deployment. RAFT optionally links cases through a case-level graph using a configurable similarity representation, and because public multi-stage troubleshooting data is extremely rare, the authors pair a synthetic benchmark built from Microsoft Learn Windows Server documentation with real Apache Jira issues carrying human-created duplicate labels. The Jira results are described as directional evidence that the advantage transfers to real case histories, and the abstract is truncated with limited full experimental detail.

RAFT addresses this by modeling each historical case as a timeline and matching the active case's current state to intermediate states in past cases.

Triplet loss is a classic metric-learning objective that trains embeddings so that an anchor is closer to a positive sample than to a negative one, and combining it with Sinkhorn-based optimal transport yields the Sinkhorn-triplet loss used here to enforce organ-wise consistency.

A new arXiv paper (2609.19213) introduces layer-wise curriculum learning for LLM compression, partitioning the model into multi-layer segments so knowledge transfer from teacher to student proceeds from easier to harder tasks. The method relies on segment-wise knowledge transfer and a feature caching scheme with multi-threading to maximize GPU utilization, and the theoretical analysis attributes convergence acceleration to controlling the cumulative error phenomenon across layers. **Background**: Knowledge distillation compresses a large teacher model into a smaller student model by training the student to mimic the teacher's outputs or internal representations, and it is commonly combined with pruning and quantization for deployment. Curriculum learning is a training strategy that orders tasks or data from easy to hard, and prior work has applied it at the layer level to improve optimization. Feature caching stores intermediate activations so they can be reused, which matters here because distilling layer by layer normally requires repeatedly recomputing or storing teacher features.

## [AR Fairness Metamodel Unifies Fairness Measure Comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19234) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19234) introduces the AR fairness metamodel, a framework that represents fairness scenarios using agents, resources, and their attributes, enabling systematic definition and comparison of fairness measures. It demonstrates the approach with discrete and continuous measures including equality, equity, group fairness, individual fairness, the Gini index, the Theil index, Jain's fairness index, and Australia's Child Care Subsidy measure, and provides formal proofs linking group fairness, individual fairness, and envy-freeness. Fairness researchers and practitioners often struggle to compare competing fairness definitions, and this metamodel offers a common vocabulary and formal basis for doing so. By making AR-based fairness definitions practical and adaptable across contexts, it could improve how AI systems are audited and how policy-driven fairness measures are implemented. The framework builds on the Tiles conceptual modeling framework, whose modular components can be connected to capture diverse fairness definitions, and an open-source implementation of Tiles is available to support fairness modeling and evaluation. The paper is a conceptual and formal contribution rather than an empirical benchmark, so its practical impact will depend on adoption by the fairness community.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Algorithmic fairness is a field concerned with ensuring that machine learning systems treat individuals and groups equitably, but researchers have proposed many incompatible fairness definitions, such as group fairness, individual fairness, and envy-freeness. A metamodel is a model of models: it provides abstract building blocks for describing many specific fairness measures in one consistent language. The AR (agent-resource) view treats fairness as a question of how resources are allocated among agents, which is a common abstraction in economics and multi-agent systems. Tiles is a framework for creating formal configurations of constraints, written in Soda and translated to Scala, that supports modular modeling of such definitions.

This paper evaluates open-source Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for zero-shot action quality assessment on Olympic diving videos using the AQA-7 benchmark, finding that standalone VLMs achieve Spearman correlations below 0.32. ## [PreDE Predicts Quantization Task Degradation Offline for World Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19441) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose PreDE (Predict Before You Deploy), a policy-calibrated framework that predicts quantization-induced task degradation for world action models (WAMs) using offline action deviations instead of costly closed-loop evaluation. Across five WAMs and four benchmark settings, PreDE issued 21 of 28 decisions before observing closed-loop outcomes (75% coverage), all matching observed acceptable or degraded labels, and on a real Franka Research 3 robot W4A4 quantization achieved a 1.37x action-query speedup with roughly 44% lower peak memory. Quantization configuration selection for WAMs currently requires exhaustive and expensive closed-loop robot evaluation, so an offline predictor that filters out degrading configurations could substantially cut deployment cost and time for robotics practitioners. The finding that task loss cannot be explained by bit width alone also challenges the common assumption that lower precision uniformly degrades performance, pointing toward policy-specific behavioral calibration. PreDE calibrates two thresholds from a small development set of closed-loop outcomes and then accepts, rejects, or defers new configurations using a fixed observation log, with decisions issued only where all thresholds consistent with development labels agree. The framework deferred some candidates that included both acceptable outcomes and a 33-percentage-point loss, and in 450 Franka Research 3 trials across two independently fine-tuned policies, all high-deviation configurations showed significant degradation while low-deviation comparisons showed no statistically significant degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: World action models (WAMs) build on video-generation backbones to predict future observations and actions for embodied AI, but their large memory and compute demands make deployment challenging. Post-training quantization reduces memory and can accelerate inference by converting model weights and activations to lower-precision formats, yet the space of bit widths, grouping schemes, and quantizer choices is huge. Closed-loop evaluation, in which a policy is actually run on a robot to see whether the task succeeds, is the gold standard for measuring task performance but is slow and costly, motivating offline prediction methods like PreDE.

## [Reproducibility Study Extends Transparent Recommender Systems with Natural-Language Profiles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19831) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv reproducibility study (2609.19831) confirms the core findings of prior work on transparent, scrutable recommender systems that use generated natural-language user profiles, and extends it with systematic context ablation, multi-seed stability across five random seeds, and a mechanistic interpretability analysis using the nnsight framework. The authors find that perturbing natural-language profiles changes predicted ratings uniformly across genres with no genre-selective effect, leaving rankings unchanged even under direct activation steering. This work strengthens the evidence base for transparent and scrutable recommender systems, which allow users to directly inspect and edit the natural-language profiles driving their recommendations, addressing cold-start and misattributed-preference issues. The negative mechanistic finding — that profile perturbations do not produce genre-selective ranking changes — points to the rating-regression objective rather than the profile interface as the bottleneck, suggesting future systems should adopt ranking objectives to realize the full scrutability promise. The study reproduces the original User Profile Recommendation (UPR) claim that it achieves competitive performance under its test-set reranking protocol, and adds multi-seed stability across five distinct random seeds for statistical reliability. The mechanistic analysis uses nnsight to probe internal model representations under counterfactual profile perturbations, and the authors trace the lack of genre-selective effects to the rating-regression objective, noting that ranking-objective models clearly exceed in this task.

## [FRESH-GEORANGE Pilot Certifies Freshness-Aware Semantic-Spatial Range Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19855) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces FRESH-GEORANGE, a semantic-spatial range retrieval design that separates source-watermark freshness from optional record age and provides deterministic query-specific recall lower bounds. A reproducible CPU pilot on 2,500 OpenFlights airport records with 740 simulated insert, delete, and text-revision events showed exact mode achieving 100.00% set recall, while the 95-percent certified mode reached 99.91% empirical mean recall with a 99.41% reported mean certificate and no observed bound violation. Geographic applications often need every object within a radius that meets a semantic threshold, but embedding indexes return approximate top-ranked lists and may silently omit qualifying records. This work matters because it offers a certification mechanism that can report a deterministic recall lower bound, which could improve trustworthiness for location-aware search and spatial databases, though the pilot validates completeness rather than performance superiority. The 95-percent certified mode had a 7.24 ms median latency, 5.85 times the 1.24 ms spatial-first exact baseline, and full-history delta replay became slower than rebuilding at larger batches. The authors note that submission-scale evaluation requires real map diffs, official recent baselines, and truly incremental versioned maintenance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic-spatial range retrieval combines geographic radius queries with semantic similarity thresholds, so users can ask for all objects near a location that also match a meaning-based condition. Traditional embedding indexes are optimized for approximate top-k ranking and do not guarantee that every qualifying record is returned. Freshness-aware retrieval adds the challenge of keeping results current as source data changes, and certified recall provides a formal lower bound on how many qualifying records were actually found.

## [Paired-History Audit Exposes Context Compression Memory Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20045) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.20045) introduces a paired-history audit that tests whether compressed memories remain sufficient after a future update, using 24 history pairs across six synthetic mechanisms, 12 memory conditions, two repeats, and two model backends. A deterministic frontier selector achieved strict reveal accuracy of 96/96 on DeepSeek and 82/96 on GLM, while a structured writer scored 62 successes with one unresolved outcome and 56/96. The work shows that a memory can answer a current query correctly yet discard distinctions needed by a later update, a failure mode that standard accuracy metrics miss. This matters for anyone building LLM memory systems, retrieval pipelines, or context compression methods, because it suggests current evaluations may overstate how well compressed context supports future reasoning. The configured four-outcome joint contrast yields finite-sample identification intervals of [0.521, 0.542] and [0.292, 0.313], which are not confidence intervals. A record-level audit found 26 and 25 well-formed but semantically wrong structured reveal memories, all 14 GLM frontier reveal failures contained correct values in the wrong wrapper, tombstone removal caused 16/16 exact replay failures, and identifier renaming dropped original frontier late-reference adequacy from 8/8 to 94/320; a label-equivariant repair preserved only 2/8 original late-reference answers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 18, 04:00

**Background**: Context compression in LLM systems reduces long histories into shorter summaries, embeddings, or soft memory tokens so that models can process them efficiently. Prior work has focused on compression ratio and current-query accuracy, but less attention has been paid to whether compressed memories preserve the distinctions needed for future updates. This paper proposes a paired-history audit, where two histories give the same current answer but require different answers after a shared future update, to directly test that sufficiency.

The paper introduces PointEvent, a lightweight event-wise state-space framework that serializes motion evidence accumulation by organizing the same event stream into locality-preserving spatiotemporal paths and chronology-preserving temporal paths, then alternating serialized scans across these complementary orders. Experiments show it achieves state-of-the-art tiny UAV detection with the fewest parameters and fastest measured inference among compared methods, with code released at https://github.com/wzz-z/PointEvent. The framework alternates serialized scans across complementary locality-preserving and chronology-preserving orders to consolidate fragmented motion evidence beyond fixed local neighborhoods, while a high-resolution event branch preserves fine-grained target responses and compact context modulation suppresses interference.
